---
name: code-review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes — Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** — does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** — does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you — run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point — a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here — not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) — fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

On top of whatever the repo documents, the Standards axis always carries the **smell baseline** below — a fixed set of Fowler code smells (_Refactoring_, ch.3) that applies even when a repo documents nothing. Two rules bind it:

- **The repo overrides.** A documented repo standard always wins; where it endorses something the baseline would flag, suppress the smell.
- **Always a judgement call.** Each smell is a labelled heuristic ("possible Feature Envy"), never a hard violation — and, like any standard here, skip anything tooling already enforces.

Each smell reads *what it is* → *how to fix*; match it against the diff:

- **Mysterious Name** — a function, variable, or type whose name doesn't reveal what it does or holds. → rename it; if no honest name comes, the design's murky.
- **Duplicated Code** — the same logic shape appears in more than one hunk or file in the change. → extract the shared shape, call it from both.
- **Feature Envy** — a method that reaches into another object's data more than its own. → move the method onto the data it envies.
- **Data Clumps** — the same few fields or params keep travelling together (a type wanting to be born). → bundle them into one type, pass that.
- **Primitive Obsession** — a primitive or string standing in for a domain concept that deserves its own type. → give the concept its own small type.
- **Repeated Switches** — the same `switch`/`if`-cascade on the same type recurs across the change. → replace with polymorphism, or one map both sites share.
- **Shotgun Surgery** — one logical change forces scattered edits across many files in the diff. → gather what changes together into one module.
- **Divergent Change** — one file or module is edited for several unrelated reasons. → split so each module changes for one reason.
- **Speculative Generality** — abstraction, parameters, or hooks added for needs the spec doesn't have. → delete it; inline back until a real need shows.
- **Message Chains** — long `a.b().c().d()` navigation the caller shouldn't depend on. → hide the walk behind one method on the first object.
- **Middle Man** — a class or function that mostly just delegates onward. → cut it, call the real target direct.
- **Refused Bequest** — a subclass or implementer that ignores or overrides most of what it inherits. → drop the inheritance, use composition.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3, **plus the smell baseline from step 3** pasted in full — the sub-agent has no other access to it.
- The brief: "Report — per file/hunk where relevant — (a) every place the diff violates a documented standard: cite the standard (file + the rule); and (b) any baseline smell you spot: name it and quote the hunk. Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls — documented-standard breaches can be hard, but baseline smells are always judgement calls, and a documented repo standard overrides the baseline. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings — the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes — that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing → **Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions → **Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

       <h1>Can a head-to-head contest tell a few known organisms apart?</h1>
       <p>Five organisms, each planted in small discs on a small tile, two at a time, with mutation switched off. The score is who holds more biomass after a fixed time. Before trusting that score, we check it against three things it must get right: a deliberately weakened copy must lose, the same organism under two names must not win by name or by position, and a contest where both sides die out must be called unreadable, not a tie.</p>
       <p class="disclosure">CPU only: small reference-simulator tiles, no GPU evidence. Local experiment, kept off the public site. This is a sensitivity check, not a tournament and not the M3 environment.</p>

import { defineConfig } from "@playwright/test";

// WebGPU in headless Chromium needs the GPU enabled explicitly.
const gpuArgs = ["--enable-unsafe-webgpu", "--enable-gpu", "--use-angle=metal", "--ignore-gpu-blocklist"];

// Worktrees run e2e side by side, each on its own port: BL_E2E_PORT (default 5174, also when empty). A port held by
// another worktree's server fails the run instead of testing that server: --strictPort makes our Vite exit, and
// readiness is our own Vite's banner, never a `url` probe (Playwright races a probe against the child's exit, so a
// foreign server could answer while our Vite is still starting). Vite binds one address family for localhost, so a
// server bound only to the other family is not detected: give each worktree its own port (another worktree's Vite on
// the same port is caught unless the two run with different Node DNS orders, --dns-result-order). Specs derive every
// origin from baseURL (testInfo.project.use.baseURL), so none is written down.
const PORT = Number(process.env.BL_E2E_PORT || 5174);
if (!Number.isInteger(PORT) || PORT < 1024 || PORT > 65535) {
  throw new Error(`BL_E2E_PORT must be an integer port from 1024 to 65535, got ${JSON.stringify(process.env.BL_E2E_PORT)}`);
}
const BASE = `http://localhost:${PORT}`;

export default defineConfig({
  testDir: "tests/e2e",
  timeout: 180_000,
  reporter: "list",
  webServer: {
    command: `node node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js apps/lab --port ${PORT} --strictPort`,
    // Vite prints "Local:   http://<host>:<port>/" after listen() (Playwright strips the colours before matching). The host
    // is localhost, or 127.0.0.1 / [::1] when Node's DNS order differs (--dns-result-order); only our own child prints here.
    wait: { stdout: new RegExp(`Local:\\s+http://(?:localhost|127\\.0\\.0\\.1|\\[::1\\]):${PORT}/`) },
    reuseExistingServer: false,
    timeout: 60_000,
  },
  use: { baseURL: BASE },
  // Extra engines need `npx playwright install firefox webkit`; enable with BL_ALL_ENGINES=1.
  projects: [
    { name: "chrome", use: { channel: "chrome", launchOptions: { args: gpuArgs } } },
    ...(process.env.BL_ALL_ENGINES
      ? [
          { name: "firefox", use: { browserName: "firefox" as const, launchOptions: { firefoxUserPrefs: { "dom.webgpu.enabled": true, "gfx.webgpu.ignore-blocklist": true } } } },
          { name: "webkit", use: { browserName: "webkit" as const } },
        ]
      : []),
  ],
});

    15	describe('archive fixture', () => {
    16	  it('the frozen fixture is internally consistent and every snapshot passed the viability screen', () => {
    17	    const { problems } = checkFixture(fixture(), CONFIG, null);
    18	    expect(problems).toEqual([]);
    19	    const fx = fixture();
    20	    expect(fx.sources.map((s) => s.short)).toEqual(CONFIG.sources.map((s) => s.short));
    21	    for (const s of fx.sources) expect(s.snapshots.length).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(CONFIG.minViableBins);
    22	    expect(fx.sources.some((s) => s.snapshots.some((x) => x.sharedWith.length > 0))).toBe(true); // shared genomes are flagged, not hidden
    23	    expect(fx.transferConditions.some((t) => t.includes('exact:false'))).toBe(true);
    24	  });
    25	  it.skipIf(!existsSync(`${RUNS}/bootstrap-m3/archive.json`))('archive fixture hexes decode and match source file SHA-256 and recomputed selection when RUNS is present', () => {
    26	    expect(checkFixture(fixture(), CONFIG, RUNS)).toEqual({ problems: [], note: null });
    27	  });
    28	  it('a viable flag that contradicts its recorded counts is rejected', () => {
    29	    const fx = fixture();
    30	    fx.sources[0].screens[0].b300 = [0, 0, 0];
    31	    expect(checkFixture(fx, CONFIG, null).problems.some((p) => p.includes('contradicts'))).toBe(true);
    32	    const fy = fixture();
    33	    fy.sources[0].screens[0].b0 = [1, 2];
    34	    expect(checkFixture(fy, CONFIG, null).problems.some((p) => p.includes('malformed'))).toBe(true);
    35	  });
    36	  it('a changed source hash or selection is rejected when RUNS is present', () => {
    37	    if (!existsSync(`${RUNS}/bootstrap-m3/archive.json`)) return;
    38	    const fx = fixture();
    39	    fx.sources[0].fileSha256 = '0'.repeat(64);
    40	    expect(checkFixture(fx, CONFIG, RUNS).problems.some((p) => p.includes('SHA-256'))).toBe(true);
    41	    const fy = fixture();
    42	    fy.sources[0].bins[2].snapshot = null;

    55	describe('mini regeneration', () => {
    56	  it('mini payload recomputes, seals, and carries the required statements', async () => {
    57	    const { cfg, fx } = mini();
    58	    const p = await generatePayload(cfg, fx, '', 1);
    59	    expect(verifySeal(p)).toBe(true);
    60	    expect(recompute(p, fx)).toEqual([]);
    61	    expect(p.statements).toEqual(STATEMENTS);
    62	    for (const s of ['CPU only', 'no GPU evidence', 'not the M3 environment', 'archive time is search time']) expect(p.statements.some((x) => x.includes(s))).toBe(true);
    63	    expect(JSON.stringify(p)).not.toMatch(/generatedAt|darwin|linux/);
    64	    expect(Object.keys(p.summary)).not.toContain('pooled');
    65	    // two seeds at 60 steps cannot validate the planted chain (K=3 floor is 1/6): the mini only checks that the machinery runs
    66	    expect(typeof p.summary.global.assayOk).toBe('boolean');
    67	    expect(p.summary.chain.fixture.id).toBe('planted-chain');
    68	    expect(p.summary.sources[0].twin.labelInvariance).toBe(true);
    69	    expect(p.summary.starved!.inventories).toHaveLength(4); // 60 steps is too short to reach the floor; the 300-step starved arena is checked below
    70	    // the verdict is recomputed from the raw runs, so an edited run record is caught by the seal and by recompute
    71	    const t = JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(p)) as Payload;
    72	    t.runs[0].cp[3][0] += 1;
    73	    expect(verifySeal(t)).toBe(false);
    74	    expect(recompute(sealPayload(t), fx).length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    75	  }, 900000);
    76	  it('worker count does not change output; mini regeneration equals itself', () => {
    77	    const a = node(['--mini', '--print', '--jobs', '1']), b = node(['--mini', '--print', '--jobs', '3']), c = node(['--mini', '--print', '--jobs', '3']);
    78	    expect(a).toBe(b);
    79	    expect(b).toBe(c);
    80	    expect(a.length).toBeGreaterThan(1000);
    81	  }, 300000);
    82	  it('the replay sample is deterministic', () => {
    83	    expect(sampled('c|a|b|bal|P1|1001', 25)).toBe(sampled('c|a|b|bal|P1|1001', 25));
    84	    const n = Array.from({ length: 1000 }, (_, i) => sampled(`k${i}`, 25)).filter(Boolean).length;
    85	    expect(n).toBeGreaterThan(150);
    86	    expect(n).toBeLessThan(350);
    87	  });
    88	});

    90	describe('freeze-check', () => {
    91	  const sh = (cwd: string, ...a: string[]) => execFileSync('git', a, { cwd, encoding: 'utf8' });
    92	  const setup = (withOutcome: boolean) => {
    93	    const dir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'frz-')) + '/';
    94	    sh(dir, 'init', '-q', '-b', 'main');
    95	    sh(dir, 'config', 'user.email', 't@t'); sh(dir, 'config', 'user.name', 't');
    96	    const put = (f: string, t: string) => { mkdirSync(dirname(dir + f), { recursive: true }); writeFileSync(dir + f, t); };
    97	    put('tools/opponents/archive-config.json', '{}\n');
    98	    put('lib.ts', 'export const x = 1;\n');
    99	    if (withOutcome) put('docs/historical-opponents-archive-2026-11-02/evidence.json', '{}\n');
   100	    sh(dir, 'add', '-A'); sh(dir, 'commit', '-q', '-m', 'freeze');
   101	    return { dir, files: ['tools/opponents/archive-config.json', 'lib.ts'], put };
   102	  };
   103	  it('freeze-check passes on a clean freeze and fails when an inventoried code file changes', () => {
   104	    const { dir, files, put } = setup(false);
   105	    expect(freezeCheck(dir, files, undefined, dir + 'none.json').problems).toEqual([]);
   106	    put('later.txt', 'outcome later\n');
   107	    sh(dir, 'add', '-A'); sh(dir, 'commit', '-q', '-m', 'outcomes after');
   108	    expect(freezeCheck(dir, files, undefined, dir + 'none.json').problems).toEqual([]);
   109	    put('lib.ts', 'export const x = 2;\n'); // one mutated library line after the freeze
   110	    const r = freezeCheck(dir, files, undefined, dir + 'none.json');
   111	    expect(r.problems.some((p) => p.includes('lib.ts changed after the freeze'))).toBe(true);
   112	    sh(dir, 'add', '-A'); sh(dir, 'commit', '-q', '-m', 'sneaky change'); // committing it does not launder it
   113	    expect(freezeCheck(dir, files, undefined, dir + 'none.json').problems.some((p) => p.includes('lib.ts changed after the freeze'))).toBe(true);
   114	  });
   115	  it('freeze-check fails when an outcome file is in the freeze commit, and when no freeze exists', () => {
   116	    const { dir, files } = setup(true);
   117	    expect(freezeCheck(dir, files, undefined, dir + 'none.json').problems.some((p) => p.includes('outcome file'))).toBe(true);
   118	    const empty = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'frz-')) + '/';
   119	    sh(empty, 'init', '-q', '-b', 'main'); sh(empty, 'config', 'user.email', 't@t'); sh(empty, 'config', 'user.name', 't');
   120	    writeFileSync(empty + 'a', '1'); sh(empty, 'add', '-A'); sh(empty, 'commit', '-q', '-m', 'x');
   121	    expect(freezeCheck(empty, [], undefined, empty + 'none.json').problems[0]).toContain('no freeze commit');
   122	  });
   123	  it('freeze-check passes on the real branch', () => {
   124	    if (!freezeCommit(REPO)) return; // before the freeze commit exists
   125	    expect(freezeCheck().problems).toEqual([]);
   126	    expect(frozenFiles().length).toBeGreaterThan(8);
   127	  });
   128	});

   141	  });
   142	  it('tampered payload, manifest, archive fixture or source is rejected; a resealed inconsistent payload is rejected', () => {
   143	    const dir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'arc-'));
   144	    const ev = join(dir, 'evidence.json'), mf = join(dir, 'manifest.json'), ar = join(dir, 'archive.json');
   145	    const p = saved();
   146	    p.runs[0].cp[3][0] += 1;
   147	    writeFileSync(ev, serialize(p));
   148	    copyFileSync(MANIFEST, mf); copyFileSync(ARCHIVE, ar);
   149	    expect(checkSaved({ evidencePath: ev, manifestPath: mf, archivePath: ar }).some((x) => x.includes('integritySha256'))).toBe(true);
   150	    // resealed but inconsistent: the summary no longer follows from the runs
   151	    writeFileSync(ev, serialize(sealPayload(p)));
   152	    const m = JSON.parse(readFileSync(MANIFEST, 'utf8'));
   153	    m.payloadFileSha256 = sha(readFileSync(ev, 'utf8')); m.integritySha256 = sealPayload(p).integritySha256;
   154	    writeFileSync(mf, JSON.stringify(m));
   155	    expect(checkSaved({ evidencePath: ev, manifestPath: mf, archivePath: ar }).some((x) => x.includes('summary differs') || x.includes('differs'))).toBe(true);
   156	    // edited archive fixture
   157	    const fx = fixture(); fx.sources[0].snapshots[0].quality += 0.1;
   158	    writeFileSync(ar, serialize(fx));
   159	    expect(checkSaved({ evidencePath: EVIDENCE, manifestPath: MANIFEST, archivePath: ar }).some((x) => x.includes('archive.json changed'))).toBe(true);
   160	    // a source tree with one changed file
   161	    const root = join(dir, 'root') + '/';
   162	    for (const f of [...sourceFiles(), 'docs/historical-opponents-2026-11-01/evidence.json']) { mkdirSync(dirname(root + f), { recursive: true }); copyFileSync(REPO + f, root + f); }
   163	    expect(checkSaved({ root, runsDir: null })).toEqual([]);
   164	    writeFileSync(root + 'packages/metrics/src/archive-tournament/index.ts', readFileSync(root + 'packages/metrics/src/archive-tournament/index.ts', 'utf8') + '\n// edit\n');
   165	    expect(checkSaved({ root, runsDir: null }).some((x) => x.includes('changed since the evidence was written'))).toBe(true);
   166	  });

    46	    ...S.sources.map((s) => ({ id: `search ${s.short}`, kind: 'measured', naive: s.tournament.analysis.naive.fraction, label: s.label, rank: s.tournament.analysis.rank.rDir, cycle: s.tournament.analysis.cycleFlag })),
    47	  ];
    48	  el('naive').querySelector('tbody')!.innerHTML = nrows.map((r) => `<tr><th scope="row">${esc(r.id)}</th><td>${esc(r.kind)}</td><td>${pct(r.naive)}</td><td>${esc(r.label)}</td><td>${rank(r.rank)}</td><td>${esc(r.cycle)}</td></tr>`).join('');
    49	}
    50	
    51	function render() {
    52	  const s = S.sources.find((x) => x.short === source.value) as Src;
    53	  const t = s.tournament, a = t.analysis;
    54	  const masked = s.label === 'control-invalid' || s.label === 'insensitive-or-biased';
    55	  const failed = [!s.twin.labelInvariance ? 'identical-organism swap not exact' : '', !s.twin.positionOk ? `identical-organism place effect too large (mean size ${s.twin.meanAbs === null ? '–' : f2(s.twin.meanAbs)} against a target of ${s.twin.effectTarget.toFixed(3)}, sign-flip p ${s.twin.signFlipP === null ? '–' : s.twin.signFlipP.toFixed(2)})` : '', !S.gw.detected ? 'weakened copy not caught' : '', !S.global.starvedOk ? 'starved arena not unreadable' : ''].filter(Boolean);
    56	  el('srchelp').textContent = `Label for this search: ${s.label}${s.underlying ? ` (underlying: ${s.underlying})` : ''}: ${LABEL_TEXT[s.label] ?? ''}. ${masked ? `The numbers below are masked by a failed control (${failed.join('; ') || 'see the control strip'}): descriptive only, no label about time. ` : ''}Cycle flag: ${a.cycleFlag}. Informative pairs: ${a.informative} of ${a.scheduled}.`;
    57	  const cells = laterVsEarlier(t.ids, t.pairs);
    58	  el('mcap').textContent = `Share of biomass the row (later) snapshot holds against the column (earlier) snapshot at step 300, with a 95% interval across seed blocks and the blocks kept. 0.50 is an even split. Uninformative pairs are shown as such and never read as a tie. Time runs down the rows and across the columns.`;
    59	  el('matrix').querySelector('thead')!.innerHTML = `<tr><th scope="col">Later against earlier</th>${t.ids.map((id) => `<th scope="col">${esc(id)}</th>`).join('')}</tr>`;
    60	  el('matrix').querySelector('tbody')!.innerHTML = cells.map((row, i) => `<tr><th scope="row">${esc(t.ids[i])}</th>${row.map((c) => `<td class="cell ${c.state}" style="background:${shade(c.value)}">${c.state === 'uninformative' ? '<strong>uninformative</strong><br>' : ''}${esc(c.text)}</td>`).join('')}</tr>`).join('');
    61	  const obs = a.rank.observed;
    62	  el('nulltext').textContent = `Directional statistic D = the mean, over informative pairs, of the later snapshot's share minus 0.5. Observed D = ${obs === null ? 'n/a' : obs.toFixed(3)}. Against all ${a.rank.perms} relabelled timelines the observed one ranks ${rank(a.rank.rDir)} (smallest possible ${rank(a.rank.minRank)}); the reverse tail is ${rank(a.rank.rRev)}. This is a rank, not a p-value. With the growing-alone residual the rank is ${rank(a.residRank?.rDir ?? null)}.`;
    63	  const h = histogram(t.permuted, obs);
    64	  const max = Math.max(...h.counts, 1), bw = 440 / h.counts.length;
    65	  el('hist').innerHTML = `<rect x="0" y="0" width="480" height="160" fill="none"/>` + h.counts.map((c, i) => `<rect class="${i === h.observedBin ? 'obs' : 'bar'}" x="${20 + i * bw}" y="${130 - (110 * c) / max}" width="${bw - 1}" height="${(110 * c) / max}"><title>${c} timelines${i === h.observedBin ? ' (contains the observed value)' : ''}</title></rect>`).join('') +
    66	    `<text x="20" y="148">${h.lo.toFixed(3)}</text><text x="460" y="148" text-anchor="end">${h.hi.toFixed(3)}</text><text x="240" y="12" text-anchor="middle">gold bar: observed D = ${obs === null ? 'n/a' : obs.toFixed(3)}</text>`;
    67	  el('histcap').textContent = `Histogram of D over all ${a.rank.perms} relabelled timelines (${t.permuted.length} with an informative statistic). The gold bar contains the observed D.`;
    68	}
    69	
    70	source.addEventListener('change', render);
    71	void archive;
    72	renderStatic();
    73	render();
     1	// Pure view helpers for the archive-tournament page. Reads numbers from evidence.json only; imports nothing from sim-ref.
     2	export const esc = (s: string): string => s.replace(/[&<>"']/g, (c) => ({ '&': '&amp;', '<': '&lt;', '>': '&gt;', '"': '&quot;', "'": '&#39;' })[c] as string);
     3	export const f2 = (x: number): string => x.toFixed(2);
     4	
     5	export interface PairView {
     6	  a: string; b: string;
     7	  summary: { complete: number; excluded: number; blocks: number; mean: number | null; interval: { lo: number; hi: number } | null; status: 'informative' | 'uninformative'; edge: string | null };
     8	}
     9	export interface Cell { state: 'self' | 'empty' | 'informative' | 'uninformative'; text: string; value: number | null }
    10	
    11	/** Matrix with rows later snapshots and columns earlier ones: the cell is the row snapshot's share against the column snapshot. */
    12	export function laterVsEarlier(ids: string[], pairs: PairView[]): Cell[][] {
    13	  const by = new Map(pairs.map((p) => [`${p.a}|${p.b}`, p]));
    14	  return ids.map((row, i) => ids.map((col, j) => {
    15	    if (i === j) return { state: 'self', text: '', value: null } as Cell;
    16	    if (j > i) return { state: 'empty', text: '', value: null } as Cell; // the column is later than the row: shown in the other triangle
    17	    const p = by.get(`${col}|${row}`) as PairView; // stored as earlier|later, payoff is the earlier one's
    18	    const s = p.summary;
    19	    const ex = s.excluded ? `, ${s.excluded} of ${s.blocks} blocks excluded` : '';
    20	    if (s.status === 'uninformative' || s.mean === null) return { state: 'uninformative', text: `uninformative (${s.complete} of ${s.blocks} blocks kept${ex ? '' : ''})`, value: null } as Cell;
    21	    const m = 1 - s.mean;
    22	    const iv = s.interval ? ` [${f2(1 - s.interval.hi)}, ${f2(1 - s.interval.lo)}]` : '';
    23	    return { state: 'informative', text: `${f2(m)}${iv}, n=${s.complete}${ex}${s.edge ? ', confirmed edge' : ''}`, value: m } as Cell;
    24	  }));
    25	}
    26	
    27	export interface Histogram { lo: number; hi: number; counts: number[]; observedBin: number }
    28	/** Histogram of the permuted statistic with the observed value's bin. */
    29	export function histogram(values: number[], observed: number | null, bins = 24): Histogram {
    30	  const lo = Math.min(...values, observed ?? Infinity), hi = Math.max(...values, observed ?? -Infinity);
    31	  const w = (hi - lo) / bins || 1;
    32	  const at = (v: number) => Math.min(bins - 1, Math.floor((v - lo) / w));
    33	  const counts = new Array(bins).fill(0);
    34	  for (const v of values) counts[at(v)]++;
    35	  return { lo, hi, counts, observedBin: observed === null ? -1 : at(observed) };
    36	}
    37	
    38	export function shade(v: number | null): string {
    39	  if (v === null) return 'transparent';
    40	  const t = Math.max(0, Math.min(1, Math.abs(v - 0.5) / 0.3));
    41	  return v >= 0.5 ? `rgba(94,160,120,${0.12 + 0.5 * t})` : `rgba(190,120,80,${0.12 + 0.5 * t})`;
    42	}
    43	export const pct = (x: number): string => `${Math.round(100 * x)}%`;
    44	export const rank = (x: number | null): string => (x === null ? 'n/a' : x.toFixed(3));

     1	# I03c Archive tournament (historical-opponents, second rung). Slice plan v2 (after Codex plan review, see REVIEW.md)
     2	
     3	Status: PLAN stage, revised 2026-11-02 after the Sol 6.1 xhigh plan review (13 findings, dispositions in REVIEW.md). v2 is a DESCRIPTIVE tournament of related surviving elites; it makes no confirmatory historical-improvement claim. Branch `claude/i03-archive-tournament` from `origin/main` 0bdd7a70. Source: "I03" in `docs/simulation-research-2026-11-01/report.md`; first rung in `docs/historical-opponents-2026-11-01/` (PR #139, merged here). Coordinator decisions D1, D3. CPU only (sim-ref), no GPU evidence, not the M3 environment, no paid calls, no deploy.
     4	
     5	## 1. Bounded question
     6	
     7	Take genomes from an archive sampled at fixed times from existing search histories, and play each later snapshot against each earlier snapshot in the first rung's CPU pairwise arena (balanced start, reciprocal positions, mutation off, genome-only standard inoculum). Does the archive show (a) directional improvement against the past, (b) cycling (a confirmed A>B>C>A), (c) a strength ordering that is not aligned with time, or (d) nothing beyond the nulls?
     8	
     9	Caveat stated everywhere: the archive "time" is the bootstrap search's **batch index** (`born` in `runs/*/archive.json` is the batch number passed to `Archive.offer` in `tools/bootstrap.ts`; observed ranges 8-79, 5-297, 5-79), not ecological time and not an evaluation count. Archives are the FINAL surviving elites (one per MAP-Elites cell, chosen by replacement), not snapshots captured at fixed historical times, and later elites are bred from earlier members, so snapshots are neither independent nor exchangeable. The sources are related histories (all three record search seed 1; byte-identical elite records recur across them), not independent replications. Every statistic below is therefore descriptive: it ranks how selected survivors relate in this arena. There is no confirmatory test, no multiplicity-corrected claim, and no inference to open-ended evolution. The search optimised survival, recovery and light dependence, not arena competition, so "later beats earlier" here is a statement about that search under this arena, not about open-ended evolution. A negative result is a valid, shippable result. The arena is also not an M3 environment; payoffs are biomass share at step 300 on a 32x32 tile.
    10	
    11	## 2. What is built (one PR)
    12	
    13	1. **Frozen archive fixture** `tools/opponents/archive-config.json` + `docs/historical-opponents-archive-2026-11-02/archive.json`. Selection is a pure function of committed inputs: for each source `runs/<name>/archive.json` (read-only from the main checkout via `RUNS`), horizon `H = max(born) + 1` batches (recorded in the fixture), `K = 5` bins of equal width over `[0, H)`; snapshot of a bin = the elite with the highest `quality` whose `born` lies in the bin and whose genome passes the viability screen (solo biomass at step 300 above the floor on **screen seeds 901..903**, disjoint from search seeds 1..300, the first rung's calibration 1..3 and confirmation 101..110, and the scoring seeds), ties by `cell` then `born`. A bin with no viable elite is recorded `empty`; a source with fewer than 4 viable bins is dropped (`dropped` is a recorded outcome, not an error). The archive is the final survivor set, so late bins may be sparse; empty bins are expected and reported. Byte-identical genomes shared across sources are flagged `shared-genome`, and a source whose snapshots are all shared with an earlier source is dropped. Sources in frozen order: `bootstrap-m3`, `bootstrap-200`, `bootstrap-div`. Compatibility is a decidable gate, not a log read: each genome must decode and pass `validateConfig`/`validateState` inside the arena config (word length, layout, bounds), and the differences between the source search config (uniform light, amplitude 160, default mu/sigma 60/20) and the arena (gradient light, amplitude 200, 154/24) are recorded in the fixture as transfer conditions. `bootstrap-200` records a legacy resume with `exact:false`; recorded as a caveat. Each snapshot is stored as genome hex (the first rung's format) with source name, source file SHA-256, cell, born, quality. No pooling of payoffs across sources and no pooled p.
    14	2. **Evidence fixtures with negative cases that fool the naive metric** (pure-function plus in-world), in `packages/metrics/src/archive-tournament/index.ts`:
    15	   - Naive metric = fraction of (later, earlier) snapshot pairs where the later one has payoff > 0.5.
    16	   - `cycle-fools-naive`: a 3-snapshot synthetic matrix A<B<C<A (times 1,2,3). Naive: 2 of 3 pairs "later wins" (0.67, reads as improvement). Sealed rule: the cycle flag is raised (the K=3 permutation floor is 1/6, so this fixture validates the cycle flag and the descriptive-rank rule, not significance rejection). A second 5-snapshot synthetic `long-cycle-fools-naive` has no triangle (edges A>B>C>D at .9, D>A at .56, E beating all at .8, chronological order D,C,B,A,E, raw rank 1/120) and must raise the cycle flag through the general directed-cycle detector, because the first rung's `findCycles` is triangles only.
    17	   - `vigour-only` (synthetic): five snapshots with solo strengths 1..5 and every payoff `V_i/(V_i+V_j)`, aligned with time. Raw rank is 1/120 and the vigour residual is zero everywhere, so the sealed label must be `directional-vigour-only`, never `directional-rank`.
    18	   - `strength-not-time` (planted; exact genomes and time assignments frozen in `archive-config.json` before scoring): the first rung's panel genomes with their committed pairwise matrix, once in strength order and once in a frozen scrambled order. Before freeze the implementer computes the exact rank of every candidate order from the committed first-rung matrix and freezes a scrambled order with rank above 0.20 (the review showed `W,F3,F2,G,F1` gives 2/120 and must not be used). The sealed rule must call the scrambled one `ordered-not-temporal` and the aligned one `directional-rank` (or `directional-vigour-only` when its residual is not also aligned). The pure-matrix version is the gate; any in-world rerun is descriptive. This is the time-shuffled null the naive metric passes.
    19	   - `planted-chain` (in-world positive control): the first rung's calibrated G and W instrument genomes and graded mixtures calibrated and frozen in `archive-config.json` with solo viability recorded; times in order. Must be `directional-rank`. Weakening of arbitrary archive genomes is descriptive only: the edit scales one generalist-specific weight toward zero and can leave a negative-weight genome less impaired (the review found this for `bootstrap-200` cell [4,1]).
    20	   - `rps-synthetic` and a transitive matrix reuse the first rung's `findCycles` fixtures. The cycle graph for archive verdicts is built from `PairSummary.edge` (first rung's full confirmation rule: every retained block on one side, at least 9 retained blocks, corrected significance, effect threshold), not `edgesFromPayoffs` mean offsets, and cycles are found by a general directed-cycle detector (new, in the archive library) that agrees with `findCycles` on triangles.
    21	3. **Controls inherited from the first rung, re-run on this archive's arena config** (same `tile 32, radius 3, steps 300`, same floor rule): identical-genome swap (a snapshot against itself under two labels: exact label invariance by integer identity, position effect below the first rung's effect target, sign-flip p > 0.05), weakened-copy (the first rung's calibrated G vs weakened-G instrument control is re-run once as the gate; the 50%-weakened copy of each source's latest snapshot is a descriptive extra with its solo viability reported, not a verdict gate), starved arena (the first rung's depletion override on one archive pair: both labels below the floor, class `both-floor`, never read as a tie).
    22	4. **Small CPU path.** Library `packages/metrics/src/archive-tournament/index.ts` (placed outside `packages/metrics/src/opponents/` because `tools/opponents/assay.ts` enumerates every file in that directory and `--check` rejects additions absent from the frozen first-rung manifest; about 350 lines: snapshot selection and viability screen, schedule, directional statistic, exact permutation null, tournament summary, verdict table). It imports `runJob`, `buildContestWorld`, `contestConfig` from `arena.ts`, and `blockPayoff`, `summarizePair`, `signFlipP`, `tInterval`, `findCycles`, `edgesFromPayoffs`, `classify` from `payoff.ts`; it does not copy them. The first rung's sources (`opponents/*`, `boot.ts`, `worker.ts`, `calibrate.ts`, `config.json`, schema, sim-ref) stay byte-identical, so its `--check` still passes. The new library, CLI and config get their own manifest source list. CLI `tools/opponents/archive.ts` reuses `boot.ts` and `worker.ts` (jobs already carry `hexA/hexB`, so the worker needs no change). Same `--write`, `--check`, `--replay-check`, `--jobs N`, `--freeze-check` contract as `assay.ts`.
    23	5. **Manifest and machine-readable evidence**: `evidence.json` (sealed canonical JSON, no timestamps or platform strings) and `manifest.json` (source hashes, archive file SHA-256, config, seeds, schedule, freeze commit).
    24	6. **Local private page** `apps/lab/opponents/archive/index.html` with `apps/lab/src/opponents/archive/{page.ts,render.ts,style.css}`: per-source time-by-time payoff matrix (rows later snapshots, columns earlier, with interval and excluded counts), the directional statistic against its permutation null (histogram of the 720 permuted values with the observed one marked), the control strip (pass/fail per control, with the weakened-copy and starved-arena cells shown), and the naive metric next to the sealed one for every fixture, so a visitor sees where the naive reading is fooled. Link from `/opponents/` back and forth. Page reads `evidence.json` only and imports nothing from sim-ref. Private: covered by the first rung's Caddy rule `^/opponents(?:/|$)` and the `Dockerfile.site` `dist/opponents` removal; the new Vite chunk is named `opponents-archive` so the existing `assets/opponents-*` removal patterns catch it.
    25	
    26	## 3. Design
    27	
    28	### 3.1 Schedule and seeds (frozen before any outcome)
    29	- Evidence seeds 1001..1010 (disjoint from search seeds 1..300 recorded in the sources, screen 901..903, first rung 101..110 and calibration 1..3). Reserve block B, seeds 2001..2010, used only if an analysis-affecting amendment follows the freeze. Balanced arm only (rare starts are known biased against the rare side, even for identical genomes, so they are not run).
    30	- Per source with K snapshots: `K(K-1)/2 = 10` ordered pairs x 10 seeds x 2 positions = 200 contests; solo vigour per snapshot x 2 masks x 10 seeds = 100 runs; controls: twin 20, weakened copy 20, starved 20. About 360 runs per source; three sources plus planted-chain (6 pairs, 120 contests) and strength-not-time (reuses the first rung's panel genomes, 10 pairs, 200 contests) is about 1500 runs. The first rung cost about 1.8 CPU-seconds per run (724 runs, 1290 CPU-s); expect about 2700 CPU-s, 6 to 12 minutes at 8 workers on a quiet machine. The 90-minute wall limit does not bound CPU (8 workers allows about 12 CPU-hours), so the budget is cumulative CPU: viability screen (about 450 s), write (about 2500 s), replay (about 2500 s), mini and regeneration tests, EVIDENCE bookkeeping; the CLI counts CPU-seconds per command and aborts past 3 CPU-hours for any one command and the slice records the total in `EVIDENCE.md` (target under 2 CPU-hours total). `uptime` is checked first and `--jobs` reduced when load > 40; replay-check may replay a frozen deterministic 25% sample of jobs plus full hash verification if the full replay would exceed the budget (recorded). Cut order if over budget: third source first, in-world strength-not-time rerun, then residual statistic.
    31	
    32	### 3.2 Statistics
    33	- Per ordered pair: block payoff, interval, sign-flip p, status `informative`/`uninformative`, exactly as the first rung (`summarizePair`, complete blocks only). Everything below is DESCRIPTIVE/exploratory. The only confirmatory gates are the instrument controls (twin, calibrated weakened copy, starved arena, planted chain, strength-not-time and cycle fixtures); there is no per-source significance test and no multiplicity claim, so the earlier Bonferroni problem (a 4-snapshot source cannot reach p below .05/3 since its minimum is 1/24) does not arise.
    34	- Directional statistic `D` = mean over (later i, earlier j) pairs of `(payoff_ij - 0.5)`, informative pairs only, count reported. Descriptive rank `r_dir = #{D_perm >= D_obs} / K!` over every permutation of the K time labels over the snapshots (K! = 120 for K = 5, 24 for a 4-snapshot source; the identity counts itself, so the minimum is 1/K!). The permutation moves each snapshot together with its whole eligibility mask and pair outcomes, so informative-pair exclusion does not change the support. It is a rank against relabelled times, not a p-value: the selected, genealogically related archive is not exchangeable, so it is reported as `r_dir` and never called significant. The reverse tail `r_rev` is reported the same way. Sub-labels use thresholds frozen in config: `r_dir <= 0.05` is `directional-rank`.
    35	- Edges for cycles use only `PairSummary.edge` confirmed edges (the first rung's full rule: every retained block on one side, at least nine retained blocks, corrected significance, effect threshold) and a general directed-cycle detector (any length), reported as a flag independent of the temporal label.
    36	- Vigour residual (descriptive): solo-growth-predicted share vs observed, as in the first rung, so a "win" that is just general vigour is flagged; the directional statistic is also reported on the residual.
    37	
    38	### 3.3 Verdict table (frozen, precedence in order; two separate outputs per source)
    39	Temporal label (one per source, first match wins):
    40	1. Any instrument control invalid (label invariance not exact, a run invalid, starved arena not `both-floor`) -> `control-invalid`.
    41	2. Twin position effect fails or the calibrated weakened-copy control is not detected (all retained blocks below 0.5, mean <= 0.40, as in the first rung's thresholds, read from its `config.json`/evidence rather than restated) -> `insensitive-or-biased`.
    42	3. Planted-chain not `directional-rank`, or `strength-not-time` scrambled not `ordered-not-temporal`, or `vigour-only` not `directional-vigour-only` (global, run once) -> `assay-failed`, no source label.
    43	4. Any scheduled pair `uninformative` (denominator is all scheduled pairs, as the first rung's gate rejects any uninformative pair) -> `partial-tournament`, with the informative count; labels 5-7 are then computed on the informative pairs and flagged partial.
    44	5. `r_dir <= 0.05`: if the vigour-residual `r_dir` is also <= 0.05 -> `directional-rank`; else `directional-vigour-only`.
    45	6. A strength ordering exists (>= 3 `PairSummary.edge` edges forming a transitive order) but `r_dir > 0.05` -> `ordered-not-temporal`.
    46	7. Otherwise -> `nothing-beyond-null`, stated as "none detected at this resolution", never "none exists".
    47	Graph flag (independent): `cycle` when the confirmed-edge digraph has a directed cycle of any length, else `acyclic`; stated alongside the temporal label, never instead of it (a source can be `directional-rank` and `cycle`; the page says so).
    48	Across sources: counts per label, no pooled rank. Related histories do not replicate one another; two sources agreeing is described as "similar in related searches", nothing stronger.
    49	
    50	### 3.4 Freeze-before-run (D3 and the stage contract)
    51	Files committed and pushed to the branch before any scoring, nothing from `evidence.json`/`manifest.json` in that commit: this PLAN, `archive-config.json` (sources, bin rule, K, seeds, floor, thresholds, verdict table version), `archive.json` snapshot fixture (selected hexes and hashes), the library and CLI code that defines the schedule and rules, and their tests on the fixtures. The viability screen uses screen seeds 901..903 only (not a scoring, calibration or search seed) and its outputs are inside the frozen `archive.json`. The freeze commit holds the PLAN, config, archive fixture, library, CLI and tests; the manifest records the freeze commit SHA and a frozen inventory (path -> SHA-256) of every scientific file (library, CLI, config, archive.json, and the first-rung files it depends on). `--freeze-check` verifies the commit is an ancestor of HEAD, that every inventoried file is byte-identical to that commit, and that no outcome files were in it; a test mutates one library line after a temp-repo freeze and the check fails. Any analysis-affecting amendment after outcomes exist requires fresh scoring seeds (block B) and marks the old result exploratory-superseded. Own Codex review (Sol 6.1, xhigh, read-only) of the freeze files happens before the freeze commit. Anything changed after the freeze is a dated amendment in `EVIDENCE.md`.
    52

   123	
   124	## 8. Non-goals and limits
   125	
   126	- No evolving against an archive, no recent-only/fixed-panel/mixture comparison, no withheld-opponent generality, no whole-patch or ecological-context transfer, no predation or reproduction claim, no open-endedness claim.
   127	- Archive time is search evaluation index; archives are surviving elites, not a full history; sources are three search runs, so intervals are wide and "nothing" means none detected at this resolution.
   128	- Biomass share at step 300 in a small CPU arena with a single chemistry; balanced arm only; no GPU or physical-hardware evidence.
   129	- No physics, schema, preset, `RULE_VERSION` or `defaultConfig()` change; no new `WorldConfig` keys; no paid calls, AWS, deploy; the plan stage commits nothing.
   130	- If scope must shrink, cut in this order: third source, `strength-not-time` in-world version (keep the pure matrix), the Playwright spec (keep render tests), residual-based statistic.
   131	
   132	## 9. Order of work
   133	
   134	1. Library with pure functions and tests (selection, permutation null, verdict, fixtures). 2. Snapshot selection and viability screen from `RUNS` into `archive.json` and `archive-config.json`. 3. CLI (`--freeze-check`, mini config). 4. Codex review of freeze files, fix, commit only freeze files, `git push -u origin claude/i03-archive-tournament`. 5. `--write` the evidence, `--replay-check`. 6. Page, packaging edits, render and e2e tests, screenshots. 7. `EVIDENCE.md` (result, deviations, cost), full gates, Codex source review.

   140	1. **Planted-chain gate (section 2.2 and verdict row 3).** v2 required the planted chain to be `directional-rank`. Selected chain: weakening percents `[80,60,40,0]`, time in ascending strength.

   144	5. **Selection outcome** (screen seeds 901..903, 13 genomes screened, all passed): all three sources kept with five viable bins each (`bootstrap-m3` horizon 80, `bootstrap-200` horizon 298, `bootstrap-div` horizon 80). The first-ranked elite of bin 2 of `bootstrap-m3` is byte-identical to bin 0 of `bootstrap-200` and bin 2 of `bootstrap-div` (flagged `shared-genome`; none of the three sources is wholly shared, so none is dropped). Elite `quality` is saturated near 1.0 for most elites, so ties (broken by cell then born) decide many bins.
   145	6. **Ordering of "ordered" strength**: `ordered` means no directed cycle among confirmed edges and at least 3 confirmed edges (a topological strength order exists).
   146	7. **Review-driven changes before freeze (Sol xhigh freeze review, 8 findings)**: the freeze inventory now includes `stats.ts`, all schema and sim-ref sources, PLAN.md and chain-calibration.json; `--check` verifies freeze provenance (ancestor, inventory, manifest commit); `--replay-check` runs the full saved-evidence and freeze verification before any simulation and refuses without evidence; the planted-chain gate requires every scheduled chain pair informative; viability flags in `archive.json` are recomputed from the recorded counts; test-only seeds 7001, 7002 replace the reserved scoring seeds in pre-freeze tests.

# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## What this is

An open-ended artificial-life ecology on WebGPU: closed in matter, open in energy, exact integer physics. Individuals, reproduction and heredity are *inferred* from local rules, never declared (no `Organism` class, no `reproduce()`). Research goal and milestones (M3 bootstrap → M4/M5 ensembles): `docs/plan.md`. Rules in prose plus the arithmetic-bounds argument: `docs/rules.md`.

The repo is a pnpm workspace (TypeScript packages + Vite lab), a Deno toolchain for headless GPU runs, and a Phoenix app (`apps/coordinator`). It is jj-colocated with git (`.jj/`).

## Commands

```bash
pnpm install
pnpm dev                 # lab at http://localhost:5173 (/selftest.html, /island.html)
bin/dev                  # lab + coordinator together (PORT, LAB_PORT override 4000/5173)
pnpm typecheck           # tsc over packages/*/src, apps/lab/src, tests (excludes tests/deno)
pnpm test                # vitest: apps/lab/test, packages/*/test, experiments/test, tools/test
pnpm vitest run packages/sim-ref/test/ref.test.ts -t "<name>"   # single test
deno run -A tests/deno/gpu_golden.ts    # GPU vs CPU reference, bit for bit (native WebGPU)
npx playwright test      # browser golden (tests/e2e), Chrome with WebGPU flags; BL_ALL_ENGINES=1 adds firefox/webkit
BL_E2E_PORT=5241 npx playwright test   # a second worktree on its own port (default 5174; strictPort, never reuses a server)
cd apps/coordinator && mix test         # or `mix test test/coordinator/queue_test.exs`
cd apps/coordinator && mix precommit    # warnings-as-errors, format, test
pnpm gen:prereg          # regenerate experiments/preregistration.md generated sections
pnpm gen:cards           # redraw the link-preview cards (site + one per world) with Chrome/WebGPU; then look at them
```

Run `pnpm install` before any `deno run` in a fresh checkout (deno 2.9 otherwise writes `workspaces` into package.json). Tests that start deno pass `--no-lock` (`tools/test/deno-no-lock.test.ts` checks; a launcher or coordinator a test starts that runs deno itself gets the PATH shim in `tests/deno/lib/no_lock_deno.ts`), but a top-level `deno run`, or a tool that starts deno, still writes a stale deno.lock: when package.json deps change, update it deliberately (`deno cache --frozen tools/stitch.ts` fails while it is stale).

`tests/deno/*.ts` are standalone scripts (not `Deno.test`); run each with `deno run -A tests/deno/<name>.ts`. Several need a real GPU; `coordinator_integration.ts` spawns `mix phx.server` against a scratch data dir.

Experiment CLIs (`tools/`, all Deno, native WebGPU): `run.ts` (write run bundles to `runs/<experiment>/<preset>/<condition>/seed-<n>/`), `analyze.ts` (ensemble analysis), `bootstrap.ts` (M3 MAP-Elites search), `retest.ts`, `calibrate.ts`, `island.ts` (headless island), `stitch.ts` (coordinator segments → bundles). Usage is in each file's header comment and in README.md. `runs/` is gitignored and can hold very large outputs.

## Architecture

**Package imports.** `@bl/*` resolve to `packages/*/src/index.ts` via four separate maps that must stay in sync: `tsconfig.json` paths, `vitest.config.ts` aliases, `deno.json` imports, and `apps/lab/vite.config.ts` aliases. There is no build step for packages.

**Two implementations of one rule set.**
- `packages/sim-ref/src/step.ts` is the executable specification (CPU).
- `packages/sim-gpu/src/shaders.ts` (WGSL) must match it bit for bit. `packages/sim-gpu/src/golden.ts` defines golden cases and runs on any WebGPU host (Deno, browser `/selftest.html`, Playwright).
- `packages/sim-ref/test/golden-hashes.test.ts` pins state hashes per `RULE_VERSION` (in `packages/schema/src/config.ts`). Any change to dynamics changes the hashes: an intended rule change bumps `RULE_VERSION` and re-pins deliberately. A change that isn't meant to alter dynamics must leave every pin untouched — new optional `WorldConfig` keys stay absent from `defaultConfig()` so existing configs hash identically.
- All arithmetic is integer (u32 matter, fixed-point controllers, counter-based PRNG keyed on seed/step/cell, stochastic rounding with remainders to the source). Conservation residual is exactly zero. Keep new arithmetic inside the bounds listed in `docs/rules.md` (`MATTER_MAX`, `POOL_MAX`, `mulShr` limits); `validateConfig`/`validateState` enforce them.

**Packages.** `schema` (config, presets, layout, genome, worlds, checkpoint encoding, migration/exchange, M3 founder set) → `sim-ref`, `sim-gpu` → `metrics` (census, tracker, collectives, activity, complexity, stats; runs on readbacks) → `runner` (conditions, `runExperiment`, observers, stitching, island client) and `search` (MAP-Elites, gates). `lineage` (schema, sim-ref) is the lineage inspector's genotype core: keys, ancestry, mutation replay, the controller probe, and the mutation-edge store the lab keeps. `runner` code runs unchanged in browsers and Deno.

**Checkpoints and replay.** A checkpoint artifact carries physics state and observer state together, under one digest. Segmented runs must equal a continuous run byte for byte (`tests/deno/segments.ts`, `tests/deno/stitch.ts`); this determinism is what makes distributed verification possible.

**Archipelago.** `apps/coordinator` (Phoenix, JSON API under `/api`, static status page `priv/static/index.html`) hands islands segment tasks. `Coordinator.Queue` does scheduling, `Coordinator.Segment` owns segment state transitions, and `Coordinator.Store` is content-addressed storage for checkpoints (`.blck`) and bundle files. A fraction of segments is re-run by a second island and compared by state hash. Islands are `apps/lab/island.html` (browser) or `tools/island.ts` (Deno), both via `packages/runner/src/island.ts`. `apps/coordinator/AGENTS.md` has Phoenix/Elixir/OTP conventions to follow there; everything between its `usage-rules` markers is generated by `mix usage_rules.sync` (config in `mix.exs`), so rerun that after dependency changes rather than editing the section. `mix usage_rules.search_docs "<term>" -p <pkg>` searches hexdocs for the coordinator's deps.

**Lab.** `apps/lab/src/sim.worker.ts` owns the WebGPU device, frame loop, rendering and OPFS storage. `apps/lab/src/execution.ts` owns census cadence, observer integrity, checkpoint settlement and replay traversal. State-changing messages run through `exclusive()`, which waits for in-flight frame execution; generation identity invalidates stale readbacks.

**Pre-registration.** `experiments/endpoints.ts` is the source of truth for primary endpoints, held-out observables and activity thresholds, and `tools/analyze.ts` executes it. The generated sections of `experiments/preregistration.md` (between `GENERATED` markers) come from `pnpm gen:prereg`, and `experiments/test/prereg-sync.test.ts` fails when they are stale. Edit `endpoints.ts` and regenerate; never hand-edit inside the markers. The doc is frozen (2026-09-27): its SHA-256 is recorded in `experiments/FROZEN`, `pnpm gen:prereg` now refuses to rewrite the generated sections, and every change goes in a dated amendment section.

**Analysis scale.** Run bundles can be hundreds of MB per run (for example `lineages.tsv`). Analysis tools stream per census rather than loading whole tables; keep new analysis code streaming.

## Codex reviews

Reviews by Codex use **Sol 6.1 at High effort**, not Astra (standing instruction from 2026-10-03, replaces the earlier `gpt-6-astra` policy). Run them read-only at phase boundaries and always before committing:

```bash
codex exec -m gpt-6.1-sol -c model_reasoning_effort=high -s read-only -C <checkout> - < prompt.md > review.txt
```

Use `< /dev/null` when the prompt is an argument (otherwise codex waits on stdin) and `-o <file>` for the final message. Historical "Astra review" comments in the code are provenance for past reviews; leave them. Delegated agents and workspaces follow the same rule.

     4	
     5	## Pre-registered before scoring (written before `--write`, 2026-11-02)
     6	
     7	- Starved arena margin: on test seeds 7001 and 7002 the 300-step starved probe of `m3:0` vs `m3:1` ended with label ratios 0.045 to 0.053 against the floor 0.0625 (1/16), a thin margin. If any of the 20 scoring runs stays above the floor, `starvedOk` is false, every source is recorded `control-invalid`, and a different depletion pair or override is a next-rung item, not a block-B amendment in this slice.
     8	- If a library bug surfaces during or after `--write`, it is recorded here and not fixed in the frozen files; the outcome stands as written, or the slice goes to block B (seeds 2001..2010) with the old result marked exploratory-superseded.
     9	- Test files are outside the freeze inventory by construction (the review's finding 1 listed them; PLAN, calibration, stats, schema and sim-ref were added, tests were not). Any post-freeze test edit is listed below as a dated amendment.

Disclosure from REVIEW.md: an early mini and arena test ran 60-step worlds with scoring seeds 1001 and 1002 before the test-only seeds were introduced; outputs were not read as outcomes.
    11	- Replay budget: `--replay-check --sample 25` (about 25% of jobs, deterministic FNV sample) plus full saved-evidence verification, unless the machine is quiet enough for a full replay; the choice is recorded below.

    13	## Result (frozen rules applied as written; seal d3d1b27f6ba1d258f3f14103490a597b867e3146a14e0d5e16aad8c06f81757b)
    14	
    15	`--write` (1360 jobs, `--jobs 6`, 2347 CPU-s, 7:55 wall): labels per source, never pooled: `bootstrap-m3` insensitive-or-biased, `bootstrap-200` insensitive-or-biased, `bootstrap-div` ordered-not-temporal. Counts: 2 insensitive-or-biased, 1 ordered-not-temporal.
    16	
    17	Global controls: runs valid; identical-genome swap exact in all three sources; calibrated weakened copy G vs W detected (mean W share 0.325, 10 of 10 blocks below 0.5, alive alone); starved arena `m3:0` vs `m3:1` both labels below the floor in all 20 runs (max ratio 0.054 against 0.0625, the pre-registered thin margin held), status uninformative; all five pure fixtures and the planted chain (six in all) passed (`assayOk` true).
    18	
    19	How to read it:
    20	- `m3` and `b200` are masked by the twin position control only: mean place effect 0.092 and 0.093 against the first rung's target 0.075 (a magnitude failure), sign-flip p 0.63 and 0.35 (no systematic direction); `div` passed it (0.057, p 0.082). The target was calibrated on the generalist; the latest archive genomes show a larger place effect in this arena. A genome-specific target or position-adjusted payoff is a next-rung item, not an amendment. No `positionAdvantage` entry is null.
    21	- `b200` numbers are masked, not a label, and descriptive only: D = 0.196, r_dir 0.033 (floor 0.008), residual rank 0.15, naive later-wins 90%, 8 confirmed edges, acyclic. Not "improved": the frozen table fires row 2 and computes no body label. This is the case where the naive metric reads 90% and the control strip stops the claim. The largest pair offset (`b200:1` vs `b200:4`, mean 0.085, offset 0.415) exceed the measured place effect (0.09) but the rule is a gate, not an adjustment.
    22	- `m3`: D -0.026, r_dir 0.69, naive 30%, 3 edges, acyclic (masked too). `div`: D -0.004, r_dir 0.55, residual rank 0.30, 4 confirmed edges, acyclic: a strength order exists and does not follow search time.
    23	- No cycle in any source: none detected at this resolution.
    24	- Weakened copies of each source's latest snapshot did not lose (mean share 0.50, 0.52, 0.48): the plan review's finding 9 realised, the photosynthesis-weight edit is generalist-specific. Descriptive only; the calibrated G/W pair carried the gate.
    25	- No fixture or control reaches `directional-rank` (strength-aligned and planted chain are `directional-vigour-only`); the top label is unvalidated.
    26	- Answer to the question: nothing shows directional improvement against the past under the frozen rules; the only strong later-beats-earlier pattern (b200) is blocked by a failed position control, one source shows a strength order unrelated to time, and no cycles were found.

    63	  const h = histogram(t.permuted, obs);
    64	  const max = Math.max(...h.counts, 1), bw = 440 / h.counts.length;
    65	  el('hist').innerHTML = `<rect x="0" y="0" width="480" height="160" fill="none"/>` + h.counts.map((c, i) => `<rect class="${i === h.observedBin ? 'obs' : 'bar'}" x="${20 + i * bw}" y="${130 - (110 * c) / max}" width="${bw - 1}" height="${(110 * c) / max}"><title>${c} timelines${i === h.observedBin ? ' (contains the observed value)' : ''}</title></rect>`).join('') +
    66	    `<text x="20" y="148">${h.lo.toFixed(3)}</text><text x="460" y="148" text-anchor="end">${h.hi.toFixed(3)}</text><text x="240" y="12" text-anchor="middle">gold bar: observed D = ${obs === null ? 'n/a' : obs.toFixed(3)}</text>`;
    67	  el('histcap').textContent = `Histogram of D over all ${a.rank.perms} relabelled timelines (${t.permuted.length} with an informative statistic). The gold bar contains the observed D.`;
    68	}

     1	// Pure view helpers for the archive-tournament page. Reads numbers from evidence.json only; imports nothing from sim-ref.
     2	export const esc = (s: string): string => s.replace(/[&<>"']/g, (c) => ({ '&': '&amp;', '<': '&lt;', '>': '&gt;', '"': '&quot;', "'": '&#39;' })[c] as string);
     3	export const f2 = (x: number): string => x.toFixed(2);
     4	
     5	export interface PairView {
     6	  a: string; b: string;
     7	  summary: { complete: number; excluded: number; blocks: number; mean: number | null; interval: { lo: number; hi: number } | null; status: 'informative' | 'uninformative'; edge: string | null };
     8	}
     9	export interface Cell { state: 'self' | 'empty' | 'informative' | 'uninformative'; text: string; value: number | null }
    10	
    11	/** Matrix with rows later snapshots and columns earlier ones: the cell is the row snapshot's share against the column snapshot. */
    12	export function laterVsEarlier(ids: string[], pairs: PairView[]): Cell[][] {
    13	  const by = new Map(pairs.map((p) => [`${p.a}|${p.b}`, p]));
    14	  return ids.map((row, i) => ids.map((col, j) => {
    15	    if (i === j) return { state: 'self', text: '', value: null } as Cell;
    16	    if (j > i) return { state: 'empty', text: '', value: null } as Cell; // the column is later than the row: shown in the other triangle
    17	    const p = by.get(`${col}|${row}`) as PairView; // stored as earlier|later, payoff is the earlier one's
    18	    const s = p.summary;
    19	    const ex = s.excluded ? `, ${s.excluded} of ${s.blocks} blocks excluded` : '';
    20	    if (s.status === 'uninformative' || s.mean === null) return { state: 'uninformative', text: `uninformative (${s.complete} of ${s.blocks} blocks kept${ex ? '' : ''})`, value: null } as Cell;
    21	    const m = 1 - s.mean;
    22	    const iv = s.interval ? ` [${f2(1 - s.interval.hi)}, ${f2(1 - s.interval.lo)}]` : '';
    23	    return { state: 'informative', text: `${f2(m)}${iv}, n=${s.complete}${ex}${s.edge ? ', confirmed edge' : ''}`, value: m } as Cell;
    24	  }));
    25	}
    26	
    27	export interface Histogram { lo: number; hi: number; counts: number[]; observedBin: number }
    28	/** Histogram of the permuted statistic with the observed value's bin. */
    29	export function histogram(values: number[], observed: number | null, bins = 24): Histogram {
    30	  const lo = Math.min(...values, observed ?? Infinity), hi = Math.max(...values, observed ?? -Infinity);
    31	  const w = (hi - lo) / bins || 1;
    32	  const at = (v: number) => Math.min(bins - 1, Math.floor((v - lo) / w));
    33	  const counts = new Array(bins).fill(0);
    34	  for (const v of values) counts[at(v)]++;
    35	  return { lo, hi, counts, observedBin: observed === null ? -1 : at(observed) };
    36	}
    37	
    38	export function shade(v: number | null): string {
    39	  if (v === null) return 'transparent';
    40	  const t = Math.max(0, Math.min(1, Math.abs(v - 0.5) / 0.3));
    41	  return v >= 0.5 ? `rgba(94,160,120,${0.12 + 0.5 * t})` : `rgba(190,120,80,${0.12 + 0.5 * t})`;
    42	}
    43	export const pct = (x: number): string => `${Math.round(100 * x)}%`;
    44	export const rank = (x: number | null): string => (x === null ? 'n/a' : x.toFixed(3));

    57	      <h2 id="caption-heading">What this does not show</h2>
    58	      <ul>
    59	        <li>Archive time is the search's batch number, not ecological time and not an evaluation count. The archive is the final surviving set, so later survivors were bred from earlier ones and are not independent.</li>
    60	        <li>The rank against shuffled timelines is a rank, not a p-value: the survivors are related and not exchangeable. Nothing here is called significant, and the three searches are not replications of one another.</li>
    61	        <li>The searches rewarded survival, recovery and light dependence, not winning contests. A contest result is biomass share at step 300 on a small tile with a different light and chemistry than the searches used.</li>
    62	        <li>"Nothing beyond the null" means none detected at this resolution. Ten seed blocks give wide intervals.</li>
    63	        <li>No fixture reaches the top label: the first rung's aligned panel and the planted chain both missed the cutoff on the growing-alone residual rank (0.09 and 0.29, against 0.05), so they are labelled vigour-only and a real "directional-rank" would be unvalidated.</li>
    64	        <li>The cycle and vigour-only fixtures are synthetic. The weakened copy of an archive organism is descriptive only.</li>
    65	      </ul>

     1	# I03c archive tournament: evidence log
     2	
     3	Freeze commit: 2a012b9fb29641351e7ead0c83fb05f316858fc8 (pushed to `origin/claude/i03-archive-tournament` before any scoring seed). Everything below is written after the freeze; nothing in the frozen inventory is edited after it (PLAN.md included). Notes, deviations and discovered bugs live here only.
     4	
     5	## Pre-registered before scoring (written before `--write`, 2026-11-02)
     6	
     7	- Starved arena margin: on test seeds 7001 and 7002 the 300-step starved probe of `m3:0` vs `m3:1` ended with label ratios 0.045 to 0.053 against the floor 0.0625 (1/16), a thin margin. If any of the 20 scoring runs stays above the floor, `starvedOk` is false, every source is recorded `control-invalid`, and a different depletion pair or override is a next-rung item, not a block-B amendment in this slice.
     8	- If a library bug surfaces during or after `--write`, it is recorded here and not fixed in the frozen files; the outcome stands as written, or the slice goes to block B (seeds 2001..2010) with the old result marked exploratory-superseded.
     9	- Test files are outside the freeze inventory by construction (the review's finding 1 listed them; PLAN, calibration, stats, schema and sim-ref were added, tests were not). Any post-freeze test edit is listed below as a dated amendment.
    10	- Cost so far: chain calibration about 910 CPU-s (7 trials, screen seeds only), viability screen 68 CPU-s, tests run before the freeze about 1000 CPU-s (two evidence-test passes, a mini triplet). Disclosure from REVIEW.md: an early mini and arena test ran 60-step worlds with scoring seeds 1001 and 1002 before the test-only seeds were introduced; outputs were not read as outcomes.
    11	- Replay budget: `--replay-check --sample 25` (about 25% of jobs, deterministic FNV sample) plus full saved-evidence verification, unless the machine is quiet enough for a full replay; the choice is recorded below.
    12	
    13	## Result (frozen rules applied as written; seal d3d1b27f6ba1d258f3f14103490a597b867e3146a14e0d5e16aad8c06f81757b)
    14	
    15	`--write` (1360 jobs, `--jobs 6`, 2347 CPU-s, 7:55 wall): labels per source, never pooled: `bootstrap-m3` insensitive-or-biased, `bootstrap-200` insensitive-or-biased, `bootstrap-div` ordered-not-temporal. Counts: 2 insensitive-or-biased, 1 ordered-not-temporal.
    16	
    17	Global controls: runs valid; identical-genome swap exact in all three sources; calibrated weakened copy G vs W detected (mean W share 0.325, 10 of 10 blocks below 0.5, alive alone); starved arena `m3:0` vs `m3:1` both labels below the floor in all 20 runs (max ratio 0.054 against 0.0625, the pre-registered thin margin held), status uninformative; all five pure fixtures and the planted chain (six in all) passed (`assayOk` true).
    18	
    19	How to read it:
    20	- `m3` and `b200` are masked by the twin position control only: mean place effect 0.092 and 0.093 against the first rung's target 0.075 (a magnitude failure), sign-flip p 0.63 and 0.35 (no systematic direction); `div` passed it (0.057, p 0.082). The target was calibrated on the generalist; the latest archive genomes show a larger place effect in this arena. A genome-specific target or position-adjusted payoff is a next-rung item, not an amendment. No `positionAdvantage` entry is null.
    21	- `b200` numbers are masked, not a label, and descriptive only: D = 0.196, r_dir 0.033 (floor 0.008), residual rank 0.15, naive later-wins 90%, 8 confirmed edges, acyclic. Not "improved": the frozen table fires row 2 and computes no body label. This is the case where the naive metric reads 90% and the control strip stops the claim. The largest pair offset (`b200:1` vs `b200:4`, mean 0.085, offset 0.415) exceed the measured place effect (0.09) but the rule is a gate, not an adjustment.
    22	- `m3`: D -0.026, r_dir 0.69, naive 30%, 3 edges, acyclic (masked too). `div`: D -0.004, r_dir 0.55, residual rank 0.30, 4 confirmed edges, acyclic: a strength order exists and does not follow search time.
    23	- No cycle in any source: none detected at this resolution.
    24	- Weakened copies of each source's latest snapshot did not lose (mean share 0.50, 0.52, 0.48): the plan review's finding 9 realised, the photosynthesis-weight edit is generalist-specific. Descriptive only; the calibrated G/W pair carried the gate.

    26	- Answer to the question: nothing shows directional improvement against the past under the frozen rules; the only strong later-beats-earlier pattern (b200) is blocked by a failed position control, one source shows a strength order unrelated to time, and no cycles were found.

# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## What this is

An open-ended artificial-life ecology on WebGPU: closed in matter, open in energy, exact integer physics. Individuals, reproduction and heredity are *inferred* from local rules, never declared (no `Organism` class, no `reproduce()`). Research goal and milestones (M3 bootstrap → M4/M5 ensembles): `docs/plan.md`. Rules in prose plus the arithmetic-bounds argument: `docs/rules.md`.

The repo is a pnpm workspace (TypeScript packages + Vite lab), a Deno toolchain for headless GPU runs, and a Phoenix app (`apps/coordinator`). It is jj-colocated with git (`.jj/`).

## Commands

```bash
pnpm install
pnpm dev                 # lab at http://localhost:5173 (/selftest.html, /island.html)
bin/dev                  # lab + coordinator together (PORT, LAB_PORT override 4000/5173)
pnpm typecheck           # tsc over packages/*/src, apps/lab/src, tests (excludes tests/deno)
pnpm test                # vitest: apps/lab/test, packages/*/test, experiments/test, tools/test
pnpm vitest run packages/sim-ref/test/ref.test.ts -t "<name>"   # single test
deno run -A tests/deno/gpu_golden.ts    # GPU vs CPU reference, bit for bit (native WebGPU)
npx playwright test      # browser golden (tests/e2e), Chrome with WebGPU flags; BL_ALL_ENGINES=1 adds firefox/webkit
BL_E2E_PORT=5241 npx playwright test   # a second worktree on its own port (default 5174; strictPort, never reuses a server)
cd apps/coordinator && mix test         # or `mix test test/coordinator/queue_test.exs`
cd apps/coordinator && mix precommit    # warnings-as-errors, format, test
pnpm gen:prereg          # regenerate experiments/preregistration.md generated sections
pnpm gen:cards           # redraw the link-preview cards (site + one per world) with Chrome/WebGPU; then look at them
```

Run `pnpm install` before any `deno run` in a fresh checkout (deno 2.9 otherwise writes `workspaces` into package.json). Tests that start deno pass `--no-lock` (`tools/test/deno-no-lock.test.ts` checks; a launcher or coordinator a test starts that runs deno itself gets the PATH shim in `tests/deno/lib/no_lock_deno.ts`), but a top-level `deno run`, or a tool that starts deno, still writes a stale deno.lock: when package.json deps change, update it deliberately (`deno cache --frozen tools/stitch.ts` fails while it is stale).

`tests/deno/*.ts` are standalone scripts (not `Deno.test`); run each with `deno run -A tests/deno/<name>.ts`. Several need a real GPU; `coordinator_integration.ts` spawns `mix phx.server` against a scratch data dir.

Experiment CLIs (`tools/`, all Deno, native WebGPU): `run.ts` (write run bundles to `runs/<experiment>/<preset>/<condition>/seed-<n>/`), `analyze.ts` (ensemble analysis), `bootstrap.ts` (M3 MAP-Elites search), `retest.ts`, `calibrate.ts`, `island.ts` (headless island), `stitch.ts` (coordinator segments → bundles). Usage is in each file's header comment and in README.md. `runs/` is gitignored and can hold very large outputs.

## Architecture

**Package imports.** `@bl/*` resolve to `packages/*/src/index.ts` via four separate maps that must stay in sync: `tsconfig.json` paths, `vitest.config.ts` aliases, `deno.json` imports, and `apps/lab/vite.config.ts` aliases. There is no build step for packages.

**Two implementations of one rule set.**
- `packages/sim-ref/src/step.ts` is the executable specification (CPU).
- `packages/sim-gpu/src/shaders.ts` (WGSL) must match it bit for bit. `packages/sim-gpu/src/golden.ts` defines golden cases and runs on any WebGPU host (Deno, browser `/selftest.html`, Playwright).
- `packages/sim-ref/test/golden-hashes.test.ts` pins state hashes per `RULE_VERSION` (in `packages/schema/src/config.ts`). Any change to dynamics changes the hashes: an intended rule change bumps `RULE_VERSION` and re-pins deliberately. A change that isn't meant to alter dynamics must leave every pin untouched — new optional `WorldConfig` keys stay absent from `defaultConfig()` so existing configs hash identically.
- All arithmetic is integer (u32 matter, fixed-point controllers, counter-based PRNG keyed on seed/step/cell, stochastic rounding with remainders to the source). Conservation residual is exactly zero. Keep new arithmetic inside the bounds listed in `docs/rules.md` (`MATTER_MAX`, `POOL_MAX`, `mulShr` limits); `validateConfig`/`validateState` enforce them.

**Packages.** `schema` (config, presets, layout, genome, worlds, checkpoint encoding, migration/exchange, M3 founder set) → `sim-ref`, `sim-gpu` → `metrics` (census, tracker, collectives, activity, complexity, stats; runs on readbacks) → `runner` (conditions, `runExperiment`, observers, stitching, island client) and `search` (MAP-Elites, gates). `lineage` (schema, sim-ref) is the lineage inspector's genotype core: keys, ancestry, mutation replay, the controller probe, and the mutation-edge store the lab keeps. `runner` code runs unchanged in browsers and Deno.

**Checkpoints and replay.** A checkpoint artifact carries physics state and observer state together, under one digest. Segmented runs must equal a continuous run byte for byte (`tests/deno/segments.ts`, `tests/deno/stitch.ts`); this determinism is what makes distributed verification possible.

**Archipelago.** `apps/coordinator` (Phoenix, JSON API under `/api`, static status page `priv/static/index.html`) hands islands segment tasks. `Coordinator.Queue` does scheduling, `Coordinator.Segment` owns segment state transitions, and `Coordinator.Store` is content-addressed storage for checkpoints (`.blck`) and bundle files. A fraction of segments is re-run by a second island and compared by state hash. Islands are `apps/lab/island.html` (browser) or `tools/island.ts` (Deno), both via `packages/runner/src/island.ts`. `apps/coordinator/AGENTS.md` has Phoenix/Elixir/OTP conventions to follow there; everything between its `usage-rules` markers is generated by `mix usage_rules.sync` (config in `mix.exs`), so rerun that after dependency changes rather than editing the section. `mix usage_rules.search_docs "<term>" -p <pkg>` searches hexdocs for the coordinator's deps.

**Lab.** `apps/lab/src/sim.worker.ts` owns the WebGPU device, frame loop, rendering and OPFS storage. `apps/lab/src/execution.ts` owns census cadence, observer integrity, checkpoint settlement and replay traversal. State-changing messages run through `exclusive()`, which waits for in-flight frame execution; generation identity invalidates stale readbacks.

**Pre-registration.** `experiments/endpoints.ts` is the source of truth for primary endpoints, held-out observables and activity thresholds, and `tools/analyze.ts` executes it. The generated sections of `experiments/preregistration.md` (between `GENERATED` markers) come from `pnpm gen:prereg`, and `experiments/test/prereg-sync.test.ts` fails when they are stale. Edit `endpoints.ts` and regenerate; never hand-edit inside the markers. The doc is frozen (2026-09-27): its SHA-256 is recorded in `experiments/FROZEN`, `pnpm gen:prereg` now refuses to rewrite the generated sections, and every change goes in a dated amendment section.

**Analysis scale.** Run bundles can be hundreds of MB per run (for example `lineages.tsv`). Analysis tools stream per census rather than loading whole tables; keep new analysis code streaming.

## Codex reviews

Reviews by Codex use **Sol 6.1 at High effort**, not Astra (standing instruction from 2026-10-03, replaces the earlier `gpt-6-astra` policy). Run them read-only at phase boundaries and always before committing:

```bash
codex exec -m gpt-6.1-sol -c model_reasoning_effort=high -s read-only -C <checkout> - < prompt.md > review.txt
```

Use `< /dev/null` when the prompt is an argument (otherwise codex waits on stdin) and `-o <file>` for the final message. Historical "Astra review" comments in the code are provenance for past reviews; leave them. Delegated agents and workspaces follow the same rule.
- `no pooled rank or p across sources`; `shared genomes across sources are flagged`; `born binning uses batch index (regression on the real archive ranges)`.
- `first rung --check still passes with the new library present` (assay.ts source enumeration unaffected).
- `seeds are disjoint from search 1..300, screen, first-rung and calibration seeds`.
- `schedule counts match config` (200 contests per source, solo, controls).
- `planted-chain in-world mini (T=60, 2 seeds) orders by weakening` and `twin snapshot swap is exact` (small real arena runs, reusing `runJob`).

`tools/test/opponents-archive-evidence.test.ts`
- `evidence seal and manifest verify` (`--check` returns no problems; no timestamps/platform strings; statements include "CPU only", "no GPU evidence", "not the M3 environment", "archive time is search time").
- `tampered payload, manifest, archive fixture or source is rejected`; `resealed but inconsistent payload is rejected`.
- `verdict in evidence equals the verdict recomputed from stored gates`.
- `freeze-check passes on the real branch and fails when an outcome file is in the freeze commit or an inventoried code file changes` (temp git repo fixture).
- `worker count does not change output` (mini config at `--jobs 1` and `3`, byte equal); `mini regeneration equals itself`.
- `archive fixture hexes decode and match source file SHA-256 when RUNS is present` (skipped with a stated reason when `runs/` is absent, since CI has no runs/).

`apps/lab/test/opponents-archive-packaging.test.ts`: Vite input present, Caddy private route matches `/opponents/archive/` and `/OPPONENTS/archive/index.html`, Dockerfile removal covers the dist path, page text carries "CPU only", "no GPU evidence", "noindex", a captioned table. `apps/lab/test/opponents-archive-render.test.ts`: matrix numbers equal the table; `uninformative` is its own state; naive and sealed metrics both shown per fixture; permutation histogram observed marker matches evidence; text says synthetic for the cycle fixture. `tests/e2e/opponents-archive.spec.ts` (Playwright, `BL_E2E_PORT=5331`): page loads from the committed evidence, no console errors, no horizontal overflow at 375 px, screenshots at 1280 and 375 saved to `docs/historical-opponents-archive-2026-11-02/` and read.
