    53	---
    54	name: code-review
    55	description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes — Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
    56	---
    57	
    58	Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:
    59	
    60	- **Standards** — does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
    61	- **Spec** — does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?
    62	
    63	Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.
    64	
    65	The issue tracker should have been provided to you — run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.
    66	
    67	## Process
    68	
    69	### 1. Pin the fixed point
    70	
    71	Whatever the user said is the fixed point — a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.
    72	
    73	Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.
    74	
    75	Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here — not inside two parallel sub-agents.
    76	
    77	### 2. Identify the spec source
    78	
    79	Look for the originating spec, in this order:
    80	
    81	1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) — fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
    82	2. A path the user passed as an argument.
    83	3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
    84	4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".
    85	
    86	### 3. Identify the standards sources
    87	
    88	Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.
    89	
    90	On top of whatever the repo documents, the Standards axis always carries the **smell baseline** below — a fixed set of Fowler code smells (_Refactoring_, ch.3) that applies even when a repo documents nothing. Two rules bind it:
    91	
    92	- **The repo overrides.** A documented repo standard always wins; where it endorses something the baseline would flag, suppress the smell.
    93	- **Always a judgement call.** Each smell is a labelled heuristic ("possible Feature Envy"), never a hard violation — and, like any standard here, skip anything tooling already enforces.
    94	
    95	Each smell reads *what it is* → *how to fix*; match it against the diff:
    96	
    97	- **Mysterious Name** — a function, variable, or type whose name doesn't reveal what it does or holds. → rename it; if no honest name comes, the design's murky.
    98	- **Duplicated Code** — the same logic shape appears in more than one hunk or file in the change. → extract the shared shape, call it from both.
    99	- **Feature Envy** — a method that reaches into another object's data more than its own. → move the method onto the data it envies.
   100	- **Data Clumps** — the same few fields or params keep travelling together (a type wanting to be born). → bundle them into one type, pass that.
   101	- **Primitive Obsession** — a primitive or string standing in for a domain concept that deserves its own type. → give the concept its own small type.
   102	- **Repeated Switches** — the same `switch`/`if`-cascade on the same type recurs across the change. → replace with polymorphism, or one map both sites share.
   103	- **Shotgun Surgery** — one logical change forces scattered edits across many files in the diff. → gather what changes together into one module.
   104	- **Divergent Change** — one file or module is edited for several unrelated reasons. → split so each module changes for one reason.
   105	- **Speculative Generality** — abstraction, parameters, or hooks added for needs the spec doesn't have. → delete it; inline back until a real need shows.
   106	- **Message Chains** — long `a.b().c().d()` navigation the caller shouldn't depend on. → hide the walk behind one method on the first object.
   107	- **Middle Man** — a class or function that mostly just delegates onward. → cut it, call the real target direct.
   108	- **Refused Bequest** — a subclass or implementer that ignores or overrides most of what it inherits. → drop the inheritance, use composition.
   109	
   110	### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel
   111	
   112	Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.
   113	
   114	**Standards sub-agent prompt** — include:
   115	
   116	- The full diff command and commit list.
   117	- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3, **plus the smell baseline from step 3** pasted in full — the sub-agent has no other access to it.
   118	- The brief: "Report — per file/hunk where relevant — (a) every place the diff violates a documented standard: cite the standard (file + the rule); and (b) any baseline smell you spot: name it and quote the hunk. Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls — documented-standard breaches can be hard, but baseline smells are always judgement calls, and a documented repo standard overrides the baseline. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."
   119	
   120	**Spec sub-agent prompt** — include:
   121	
   122	- The diff command and commit list.
   123	- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
   124	- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."
   125	
   126	If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.
   127	
   128	### 5. Aggregate
   129	
   130	Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings — the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).
   131	
   132	End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes — that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.
   133	
   134	## Why two axes
   135	
   136	A change can pass one axis and fail the other:
   137	
   138	- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing → **Standards pass, Spec fail.**
   139	- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions → **Spec pass, Standards fail.**
   140	
   141	Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

   299	       <h1>Can a head-to-head contest tell a few known organisms apart?</h1>
   300	       <p>Five organisms, each planted in small discs on a small tile, two at a time, with mutation switched off. The score is who holds more biomass after a fixed time. Before trusting that score, we check it against three things it must get right: a deliberately weakened copy must lose, the same organism under two names must not win by name or by position, and a contest where both sides die out must be called unreadable, not a tie.</p>
   301	       <p class="disclosure">CPU only: small reference-simulator tiles, no GPU evidence. Local experiment, kept off the public site. This is a sensitivity check, not a tournament and not the M3 environment.</p>

   452	// Worktrees run e2e side by side, each on its own port: BL_E2E_PORT (default 5174, also when empty). A port held by
   453	// another worktree's server fails the run instead of testing that server: --strictPort makes our Vite exit, and
   454	// readiness is our own Vite's banner, never a `url` probe (Playwright races a probe against the child's exit, so a
   455	// foreign server could answer while our Vite is still starting). Vite binds one address family for localhost, so a
   456	// server bound only to the other family is not detected: give each worktree its own port (another worktree's Vite on
   457	// the same port is caught unless the two run with different Node DNS orders, --dns-result-order). Specs derive every
   458	// origin from baseURL (testInfo.project.use.baseURL), so none is written down.
   459	const PORT = Number(process.env.BL_E2E_PORT || 5174);
   460	if (!Number.isInteger(PORT) || PORT < 1024 || PORT > 65535) {
   461	  throw new Error(`BL_E2E_PORT must be an integer port from 1024 to 65535, got ${JSON.stringify(process.env.BL_E2E_PORT)}`);
   462	}
   463	const BASE = `http://localhost:${PORT}`;

   472	    // is localhost, or 127.0.0.1 / [::1] when Node's DNS order differs (--dns-result-order); only our own child prints here.
   473	    wait: { stdout: new RegExp(`Local:\\s+http://(?:localhost|127\\.0\\.0\\.1|\\[::1\\]):${PORT}/`) },
   474	    reuseExistingServer: false,

   553	    65	    // two seeds at 60 steps cannot validate the planted chain (K=3 floor is 1/6): the mini only checks that the machinery runs

   558	    70	    // the verdict is recomputed from the raw runs, so an edited run record is caught by the seal and by recompute

   561	    73	    expect(verifySeal(t)).toBe(false);
   562	    74	    expect(recompute(sealPayload(t), fx).length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
   563	    75	  }, 900000);

   578	    90	describe('freeze-check', () => {
   579	    91	  const sh = (cwd: string, ...a: string[]) => execFileSync('git', a, { cwd, encoding: 'utf8' });
   580	    92	  const setup = (withOutcome: boolean) => {
   581	    93	    const dir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'frz-')) + '/';
   582	    94	    sh(dir, 'init', '-q', '-b', 'main');
   583	    95	    sh(dir, 'config', 'user.email', 't@t'); sh(dir, 'config', 'user.name', 't');
   584	    96	    const put = (f: string, t: string) => { mkdirSync(dirname(dir + f), { recursive: true }); writeFileSync(dir + f, t); };
   585	    97	    put('tools/opponents/archive-config.json', '{}\n');
   586	    98	    put('lib.ts', 'export const x = 1;\n');
   587	    99	    if (withOutcome) put('docs/historical-opponents-archive-2026-11-02/evidence.json', '{}\n');
   588	   100	    sh(dir, 'add', '-A'); sh(dir, 'commit', '-q', '-m', 'freeze');
   589	   101	    return { dir, files: ['tools/opponents/archive-config.json', 'lib.ts'], put };
   590	   102	  };
   591	   103	  it('freeze-check passes on a clean freeze and fails when an inventoried code file changes', () => {
   592	   104	    const { dir, files, put } = setup(false);
   593	   105	    expect(freezeCheck(dir, files, undefined, dir + 'none.json').problems).toEqual([]);
   594	   106	    put('later.txt', 'outcome later\n');
   595	   107	    sh(dir, 'add', '-A'); sh(dir, 'commit', '-q', '-m', 'outcomes after');
   596	   108	    expect(freezeCheck(dir, files, undefined, dir + 'none.json').problems).toEqual([]);
   597	   109	    put('lib.ts', 'export const x = 2;\n'); // one mutated library line after the freeze
   598	   110	    const r = freezeCheck(dir, files, undefined, dir + 'none.json');
   599	   111	    expect(r.problems.some((p) => p.includes('lib.ts changed after the freeze'))).toBe(true);
   600	   112	    sh(dir, 'add', '-A'); sh(dir, 'commit', '-q', '-m', 'sneaky change'); // committing it does not launder it
   601	   113	    expect(freezeCheck(dir, files, undefined, dir + 'none.json').problems.some((p) => p.includes('lib.ts changed after the freeze'))).toBe(true);
   602	   114	  });
   603	   115	  it('freeze-check fails when an outcome file is in the freeze commit, and when no freeze exists', () => {
   604	   116	    const { dir, files } = setup(true);
   605	   117	    expect(freezeCheck(dir, files, undefined, dir + 'none.json').problems.some((p) => p.includes('outcome file'))).toBe(true);
   606	   118	    const empty = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'frz-')) + '/';
   607	   119	    sh(empty, 'init', '-q', '-b', 'main'); sh(empty, 'config', 'user.email', 't@t'); sh(empty, 'config', 'user.name', 't');
   608	   120	    writeFileSync(empty + 'a', '1'); sh(empty, 'add', '-A'); sh(empty, 'commit', '-q', '-m', 'x');
   609	   121	    expect(freezeCheck(empty, [], undefined, empty + 'none.json').problems[0]).toContain('no freeze commit');
   610	   122	  });
   611	   123	  it('freeze-check passes on the real branch', () => {
   612	   124	    if (!freezeCommit(REPO)) return; // before the freeze commit exists
   613	   125	    expect(freezeCheck().problems).toEqual([]);
   614	   126	    expect(frozenFiles().length).toBeGreaterThan(8);
   615	   127	  });
   616	   128	});

   669	     5	const el = <T extends HTMLElement>(id: string) => document.getElementById(id) as T;
   670	     6	interface Analysis { n: number; scheduled: number; informative: number; naive: { wins: number; pairs: number; fraction: number }; rank: { observed: number | null; rDir: number | null; rRev: number | null; perms: number; minRank: number; terms: number }; residRank: { rDir: number | null } | null; edgeCount: number; cycleFlag: string; ordered: boolean }
   671	     7	interface Tour { ids: string[]; pairs: PairView[]; analysis: Analysis; permuted: number[] }
   672	     8	interface Fix { id: string; synthetic: boolean; naive: { fraction: number; wins: number; pairs: number }; rDir: number | null; minRank: number; cycleFlag: string; label: string; expected: string; pass: boolean; note: string }
   673	     9	interface Src { short: string; name: string; tournament: Tour; twin: { labelInvariance: boolean; positionAdvantage: (number | null)[]; signFlipP: number | null; meanAbs: number | null; effectTarget: number; positionOk: boolean }; weakCopy: { complete: number; meanPayoffB: number | null; soloAboveFloor: boolean | null; detected: boolean }; label: string; underlying: string | null }
   674	    10	const S = evidence.summary as unknown as {

   695	    31	function renderStatic() {
   696	    32	  const counts = Object.entries(S.labelCounts).filter(([, n]) => n > 0).map(([l, n]) => `${n} ${l}`).join(', ');
   697	    33	  el('verdict').className = 'verdict warn';
   698	    34	  el('verdict').textContent = `Recorded labels, one per search, never pooled: ${S.sources.map((s) => `${s.short} ${s.label}${s.underlying ? ` (underlying ${s.underlying})` : ''}`).join('; ')}. Counts: ${counts}. Related searches that agree are similar, not replicated. Any outcome is a valid result and is shown as it came out.`;
   699	    35	  const rows: [string, string, string, boolean | null][] = [
   700	    36	    ['Same organism, two names: exact', 'Swapping the labels changes nothing physical: counts swap exactly.', S.sources.map((s) => `${s.short}: ${s.twin.labelInvariance ? 'exact' : 'differs'}`).join('; '), S.sources.every((s) => s.twin.labelInvariance)],
   701	    37	    ['Same organism, two names: position', 'No systematic gain from position (sign-flip p above 0.05) and a mean place effect below the first rung\'s target.', S.sources.map((s) => `${s.short}: mean size ${s.twin.meanAbs === null ? '–' : f2(s.twin.meanAbs)}, p ${s.twin.signFlipP === null ? '–' : s.twin.signFlipP.toFixed(3)}`).join('; '), S.sources.every((s) => s.twin.positionOk)],
   702	    38	    ['Weakened copy must lose (calibrated pair)', 'The generalist with half its photosynthesis weight loses in every block and holds at most 0.40.', `mean share of the weakened copy ${S.gw.meanPayoffB === null ? '–' : f2(S.gw.meanPayoffB)} over ${S.gw.complete} blocks; alive alone: ${S.gw.soloAboveFloor ? 'yes' : 'no'}`, S.gw.detected],
   703	    39	    ['Weakened copy of each search\'s latest (descriptive)', 'Shown, not gated: the edit can leave some genomes less impaired.', S.sources.map((s) => `${s.short}: mean share ${s.weakCopy.meanPayoffB === null ? '–' : f2(s.weakCopy.meanPayoffB)}, alive alone ${s.weakCopy.soloAboveFloor ? 'yes' : 'no'}, caught ${s.weakCopy.detected ? 'yes' : 'no'}`).join('; '), null],
   704	    40	    ['Starved arena is not a tie', 'A dark decaying tile must end with both sides at the floor and be called unreadable.', S.starved ? `${S.starved.pair.join(' vs ')}: ${S.starved.bothBelowFloorEveryRun ?    705	    41	    ['Planted chain', 'Graded weakening of one organism, in order, must be called directional (rank or vigour-only).', `${S.chain.fixture.label}; rank ${rank(S.chain.fixture.rDir)}`, S.chain.fixture.pass],
   706	    42	  ];

   708	    44	  const nrows = [

   711	    47	  ];

   715	    51	function render() {
   716	    52	  const s = S.sources.find((x) => x.short === source.value) as Src;
   717	    53	  const t = s.tournament, a = t.analysis;
   718	    54	  const masked = s.label === 'control-invalid' || s.label === 'insensitive-or-biased';
   719	    55	  const failed = [!s.twin.labelInvariance ? 'identical-organism swap not exact' : '', !s.twin.positionOk ? `identical-organism place effect too large (mean size ${s.twin.meanAbs === null ? '–' : f2(s.twin.meanAbs)} against a target of ${s.twin.effectTarget.toFixed(3)}, sign-flip p ${s.twin.signFlipP === null ? '–' : s.twin.signFlipP.toFixed(2)})` : '', !S.gw.detected ? 'weakened copy not caught' : '', !S.global.starvedOk ? 'starved arena not unreadable' : ''].filter(Boolean);
   720	    56	  el('srchelp').textContent = `Label for this search: ${s.label}${s.underlying ? ` (underlying: ${s.underlying})` : ''}: ${LABEL_TEXT[s.label] ?? ''}. ${masked ? `The numbers below are masked by a failed control (${failed.join('; ') || 'see the control strip'}): descriptive only, no label about time. ` : ''}Cycle flag: ${a.cycleFlag}. Informative pairs: ${a.informative} of ${a.scheduled}.`;
   721	    57	  const cells = laterVsEarlier(t.ids, t.pairs);
   722	    58	  el('mcap').textContent = `Share of biomass the row (later) snapshot holds against the column (earlier) snapshot at step 300, with a 95% interval across seed blocks and the blocks kept. 0.50 is an even split. Uninformative pairs are shown as such and never read as a tie. Time runs down the rows and across the columns.`;
   723	    59	  el('matrix').querySelector('thead')!.innerHTML = `<tr><th scope="col">Later against earlier</th>${t.ids.map((id) => `<th scope="col">${esc(id)}</th>`).join('')}</tr>`;
   724	    60	  el('matrix').querySelector('tbody')!.innerHTML = cells.map((row, i) => `<tr><th scope="row">${esc(t.ids[i])}</th>${row.map((c) => `<td class="cell ${c.state}" style="background:${shade(c.value)}">${c.state === 'uninformative' ? '<strong>uninformative</strong><br>' : ''}${esc(c.text)}</td>`).join('')}</tr>`).join('');
   725	    61	  const obs = a.rank.observed;
   726	    62	  el('nulltext').textContent = `Directional statistic D = the mean, over informative pairs, of the later snapshot's share minus 0.5. Observed D = ${obs === null ? 'n/a' : obs.toFixed(3)}. Against all ${a.rank.perms} relabelled timelines the observed one ranks ${rank(a.rank.rDir)} (smallest possible ${rank(a.rank.minRank)}); the reverse tail is ${rank(a.rank.rRev)}. This is a rank, not a p-value. With the growing-alone residual the rank is ${rank(a.residRank?.rDir ?? null)}.`;
   727	    63	  const h = histogram(t.permuted, obs);
   728	    64	  const max = Math.max(...h.counts, 1), bw = 440 / h.counts.length;
   729	    65	  el('hist').innerHTML = `<rect x="0" y="0" width="480" height="160" fill="none"/>` + h.counts.map((c, i) => `<rect class="${i === h.observedBin ? 'obs' : 'bar'}" x="${20 + i * bw}" y="${130 - (110 * c) / max}" width="${bw - 1}" height="${(110 * c) / max}"><title>${c} timelines${i === h.observedBin ? ' (contains the observed value)' : ''}</title></rect>`).join('') +
   730	    66	    `<text x="20" y="148">${h.lo.toFixed(3)}</text><text x="460" y="148" text-anchor="end">${h.hi.toFixed(3)}</text><text x="240" y="12" text-anchor="middle">gold bar: observed D = ${obs === null ? 'n/a' : obs.toFixed(3)}</text>`;
   731	    67	  el('histcap').textContent = `Histogram of D over all ${a.rank.perms} relabelled timelines (${t.permuted.length} with an informative statistic). The gold bar contains the observed D.`;
   732	    68	}

   738	     1	// Pure view helpers for the archive-tournament page. Reads numbers from evidence.json only; imports nothing from sim-ref.
   739	     2	export const esc = (s: string): string => s.replace(/[&<>"']/g, (c) => ({ '&': '&amp;', '<': '&lt;', '>': '&gt;', '"': '&quot;', "'": '&#39;' })[c] as string);
   740	     3	export const f2 = (x: number): string => x.toFixed(2);
   741	     4	
   742	     5	export interface PairView {
   743	     6	  a: string; b: string;
   744	     7	  summary: { complete: number; excluded: number; blocks: number; mean: number | null; interval: { lo: number; hi: number } | null; status: 'informative' | 'uninformative'; edge: string | null };
   745	     8	}
   746	     9	export interface Cell { state: 'self' | 'empty' | 'informative' | 'uninformative'; text: string; value: number | null }
   747	    10	
   748	    11	/** Matrix with rows later snapshots and columns earlier ones: the cell is the row snapshot's share against the column snapshot. */
   749	    12	export function laterVsEarlier(ids: string[], pairs: PairView[]): Cell[][] {
   750	    13	  const by = new Map(pairs.map((p) => [`${p.a}|${p.b}`, p]));
   751	    14	  return ids.map((row, i) => ids.map((col, j) => {
   752	    15	    if (i === j) return { state: 'self', text: '', value: null } as Cell;
   753	    16	    if (j > i) return { state: 'empty', text: '', value: null } as Cell; // the column is later than the row: shown in the other triangle
   754	    17	    const p = by.get(`${col}|${row}`) as PairView; // stored as earlier|later, payoff is the earlier one's
   755	    18	    const s = p.summary;
   756	    19	    const ex = s.excluded ? `, ${s.excluded} of ${s.blocks} blocks excluded` : '';
   757	    20	    if (s.status === 'uninformative' || s.mean === null) return { state: 'uninformative', text: `uninformative (${s.complete} of ${s.blocks} blocks kept${ex ? '' : ''})`, value: null } as Cell;
   758	    21	    const m = 1 - s.mean;
   759	    22	    const iv = s.interval ? ` [${f2(1 - s.interval.hi)}, ${f2(1 - s.interval.lo)}]` : '';
   760	    23	    return { state: 'informative', text: `${f2(m)}${iv}, n=${s.complete}${ex}${s.edge ? ', confirmed edge' : ''}`, value: m } as Cell;
   761	    24	  }));
   762	    25	}
   763	    26	
   764	    27	export interface Histogram { lo: number; hi: number; counts: number[]; observedBin: number }
   765	    28	/** Histogram of the permuted statistic with the observed value's bin. */
   766	    29	export function histogram(values: number[], observed: number | null, bins = 24): Histogram {
   767	    30	  const lo = Math.min(...values, observed ?? Infinity), hi = Math.max(...values, observed ?? -Infinity);
   768	    31	  const w = (hi - lo) / bins || 1;
   769	    32	  const at = (v: number) => Math.min(bins - 1, Math.floor((v - lo) / w));
   770	    33	  const counts = new Array(bins).fill(0);
   771	    34	  for (const v of values) counts[at(v)]++;
   772	    35	  return { lo, hi, counts, observedBin: observed === null ? -1 : at(observed) };
   773	    36	}
   774	    37	
   775	    38	export function shade(v: number | null): string {
   776	    39	  if (v === null) return 'transparent';
   777	    40	  const t = Math.max(0, Math.min(1, Math.abs(v - 0.5) / 0.3));
   778	    41	  return v >= 0.5 ? `rgba(94,160,120,${0.12 + 0.5 * t})` : `rgba(190,120,80,${0.12 + 0.5 * t})`;
   779	    42	}
   780	    43	export const pct = (x: number): string => `${Math.round(100 * x)}%`;
   781	    44	export const rank = (x: number | null): string => (x === null ? 'n/a' : x.toFixed(3));

   788	     7	td.informative{white-space:normal}

   806	    17	      <p>Three searches each kept one best surviving organism per cell. We took five of them per search, spread over the search's own timeline, and played every later one against every earlier one in the first rung's small contest arena. We then ask whether the later ones win more than a shuffled timeline would say, whether the wins go in circles, or whether there is nothing beyond what shuffling gives.</p>
   807	    18	      <p class="disclosure">CPU only, no GPU evidence, not the M3 environment. Local experiment, kept off the public site (noindex). There is no significance claim and no pooled number.</p>

   846	    57	      <h2 id="caption-heading">What this does not show</h2>
   847	    58	      <ul>
   848	    59	        <li>Archive time is the search's batch number, not ecological time and not an evaluation count. The archive is the final surviving set, so later survivors were bred from earlier ones and are not independent.</li>
   849	    60	        <li>The rank against shuffled timelines is a rank, not a p-value: the survivors are related and not exchangeable. Nothing here is called significant, and the three searches are not replications of one another.</li>
   850	    61	        <li>The searches rewarded survival, recovery and light dependence, not winning contests. A contest result is biomass share at step 300 on a small tile with a different light and chemistry than the searches used.</li>
   851	    62	        <li>"Nothing beyond the null" means none detected at this resolution. Ten seed blocks give wide intervals.</li>
   852	    63	        <li>No fixture reaches the top label: the first rung's aligned panel and the planted chain both missed the cutoff on the growing-alone residual rank (0.09 and 0.29, against 0.05), so they are labelled vigour-only and a real "directional-rank" would be unvalidated.</li>
   853	    64	        <li>The cycle and vigour-only fixtures are synthetic. The weakened copy of an archive organism is descriptive only.</li>
   854	    65	      </ul>

   862	 succeeded in 0ms:
   863	     1	# I03c Archive tournament (historical-opponents, second rung). Slice plan v2 (after Codex plan review, see REVIEW.md)
   864	     2	
   865	     3	Status: PLAN stage, revised 2026-11-02 after the Sol 6.1 xhigh plan review (13 findings, dispositions in REVIEW.md). v2 is a DESCRIPTIVE tournament of related surviving elites; it makes no confirmatory historical-improvement claim. Branch `claude/i03-archive-tournament` from `origin/main` 0bdd7a70. Source: "I03" in `docs/simulation-research-2026-11-01/report.md`; first rung in `docs/historical-opponents-2026-11-01/` (PR #139, merged here). Coordinator decisions D1, D3. CPU only (sim-ref), no GPU evidence, not the M3 environment, no paid calls, no deploy.
   866	     4	
   867	     5	## 1. Bounded question
   868	     6	
   869	     7	Take genomes from an archive sampled at fixed times from existing search histories, and play each later snapshot against each earlier snapshot in the first rung's CPU pairwise arena (balanced start, reciprocal positions, mutation off, genome-only standard inoculum). Does the archive show (a) directional improvement against the past, (b) cycling (a confirmed A>B>C>A), (c) a strength ordering that is not aligned with time, or (d) nothing beyond the nulls?
   870	     8	
   871	     9	Caveat stated everywhere: the archive "time" is the bootstrap search's **batch index** (`born` in `runs/*/archive.json` is the batch number passed to `Archive.offer` in `tools/bootstrap.ts`; observed ranges 8-79, 5-297, 5-79), not ecological time and not an evaluation count. Archives are the FINAL surviving elites (one per MAP-Elites cell, chosen by replacement), not snapshots captured at fixed historical times, and later elites are bred from earlier members, so snapshots are neither independent nor exchangeable. The sources are related histories (all three record search seed 1; byte-identical elite records recur across them), not independent replications. Every statistic below is therefore descriptive: it ranks how selected survivors relate in this arena. There is no confirmatory test, no multiplicity-corrected claim, and no inference to open-ended evolution. The search optimised survival, recovery and light dependence, not arena competition, so "later beats earlier" here is a statement about that search under this arena, not about open-ended evolution. A negative result is a valid, shippable result. The arena is also not an M3 environment; payoffs are biomass share at step 300 on a 32x32 tile.
   872	    10	
   873	    11	## 2. What is built (one PR)
   874	    12	
   875	    13	1. **Frozen archive fixture** `tools/opponents/archive-config.json` + `docs/historical-opponents-archive-2026-11-02/archive.json`. Selection is a pure function of committed inputs: for each source `runs/<name>/archive.json` (read-only from the main checkout via `RUNS`), horizon `H = max(born) + 1` batches (recorded in the fixture), `K = 5` bins of equal width over `[0, H)`; snapshot of a bin = the elite with the highest `quality` whose `born` lies in the bin and whose genome passes the viability screen (solo biomass at step 300 above the floor on **screen seeds 901..903**, disjoint from search seeds 1..300, the first rung's calibration 1..3 and confirmation 101..110, and the scoring seeds), ties by `cell` then `born`. A bin with no viable elite is recorded `empty`; a source with fewer than 4 viable bins is dropped (`dropped` is a recorded outcome, not an error). The archive is the final survivor set, so late bins may be sparse; empty bins are expected and reported. Byte-identical genomes shared across sources are flagged `shared-genome`, and a source whose snapshots are all shared with an earlier source is dropped. Sources in frozen order: `bootstrap-m3`, `bootstrap-200`, `bootstrap-div`. Compatibility is a decidable gate, not a log read: each genome must decode and pass `validateConfig`/`validateState` inside the arena config (word length, layout, bounds), and the differences between the source search config (uniform light, amplitude 160, default mu/sigma 60/20) and the arena (gradient light, amplitude 200, 154/24) are recorded in the fixture as transfer conditions. `bootstrap-200` records a legacy resume with `exact:false`; recorded as a caveat. Each snapshot is stored as genome hex (the first rung's format) with source name, source file SHA-256, cell, born, quality. No pooling of payoffs across sources and no pooled p.
   876	    14	2. **Evidence fixtures with negative cases that fool the naive metric** (pure-function plus in-world), in `packages/metrics/src/archive-tournament/index.ts`:
   877	    15	   - Naive metric = fraction of (later, earlier) snapshot pairs where the later one has payoff > 0.5.
   878	    16	   - `cycle-fools-naive`: a 3-snapshot synthetic matrix A<B<C<A (times 1,2,3). Naive: 2 of 3 pairs "later wins" (0.67, reads as improvement). Sealed rule: the cycle flag is raised (the K=3 permutation floor is 1/6, so this fixture validates the cycle flag and the descriptive-rank rule, not significance rejection). A second 5-snapshot synthetic `long-cycle-fools-naive` has no triangle (edges A>B>C>D at .9, D>A at .56, E beating all at .8, chronological order D,C,B,A,E, raw rank 1/120) and must raise the cycle flag through the general directed-cycle detector, because the first rung's `findCycles` is triangles only.
   879	    17	   - `vigour-only` (synthetic): five snapshots with solo strengths 1..5 and every payoff `V_i/(V_i+V_j)`, aligned with time. Raw rank is 1/120 and the vigour residual is zero everywhere, so the sealed label must be `directional-vigour-only`, never `directional-rank`.
   880	    18	   - `strength-not-time` (planted; exact genomes and time assignments frozen in `archive-config.json` before scoring): the first rung's panel genomes with their committed pairwise matrix, once in strength order and once in a frozen scrambled order. Before freeze the implementer computes the exact rank of every candidate order from the committed first-rung matrix and freezes a scrambled order with rank above 0.20 (the review showed `W,F3,F2,G,F1` gives 2/120 and must not be used). The sealed rule must call the scrambled one `ordered-not-temporal` and the aligned one `directional-rank` (or `directional-vigour-only` when its residual is not also aligned). The pure-matrix version is the gate; any in-world rerun is descriptive. This is the time-shuffled null the naive metric passes.
   881	    19	   - `planted-chain` (in-world positive control): the first rung's calibrated G and W instrument genomes and graded mixtures calibrated and frozen in `archive-config.json` with solo viability recorded; times in order. Must be `directional-rank`. Weakening of arbitrary archive genomes is descriptive only: the edit scales one generalist-specific weight toward zero and can leave a negative-weight genome less impaired (the review found this for `bootstrap-200` cell [4,1]).
   882	    20	   - `rps-synthetic` and a transitive matrix reuse the first rung's `findCycles` fixtures. The cycle graph for archive verdicts is built from `PairSummary.edge` (first rung's full confirmation rule: every retained block on one side, at least 9 retained blocks, corrected significance, effect threshold), not `edgesFromPayoffs` mean offsets, and cycles are found by a general directed-cycle detector (new, in the archive library) that agrees with `findCycles` on triangles.
   883	    21	3. **Controls inherited from the first rung, re-run on this archive's arena config** (same `tile 32, radius 3, steps 300`, same floor rule): identical-genome swap (a snapshot against itself under two labels: exact label invariance by integer identity, position effect below the first rung's effect target, sign-flip p > 0.05), weakened-copy (the first rung's calibrated G vs weakened-G instrument control is re-run once as the gate; the 50%-weakened copy of each source's latest snapshot is a descriptive extra with its solo viability reported, not a verdict gate), starved arena (the first rung's depletion override on one archive pair: both labels below the floor, class `both-floor`, never read as a tie).
   884	    22	4. **Small CPU path.** Library `packages/metrics/src/archive-tournament/index.ts` (placed outside `packages/metrics/src/opponents/` because `tools/opponents/assay.ts` enumerates every file in that directory and `--check` rejects additions absent from the frozen first-rung manifest; about 350 lines: snapshot selection and viability screen, schedule, directional statistic, exact permutation null, tournament summary, verdict table). It imports `runJob`, `buildContestWorld`, `contestConfig` from `arena.ts`, and `blockPayoff`, `summarizePair`, `signFlipP`, `tInterval`, `findCycles`, `edgesFromPayoffs`, `classify` from `payoff.ts`; it does not copy them. The first rung's sources (`opponents/*`, `boot.ts`, `worker.ts`, `calibrate.ts`, `config.json`, schema, sim-ref) stay byte-identical, so its `--check` still passes. The new library, CLI and config get their own manifest source list. CLI `tools/opponents/archive.ts` reuses `boot.ts` and `worker.ts` (jobs already carry `hexA/hexB`, so the worker needs no change). Same `--write`, `--check`, `--replay-check`, `--jobs N`, `--freeze-check` contract as `assay.ts`.
   885	    23	5. **Manifest and machine-readable evidence**: `evidence.json` (sealed canonical JSON, no timestamps or platform strings) and `manifest.json` (source hashes, archive file SHA-256, config, seeds, schedule, freeze commit).
   886	    24	6. **Local private page** `apps/lab/opponents/archive/index.html` with `apps/lab/src/opponents/archive/{page.ts,render.ts,style.css}`: per-source time-by-time payoff matrix (rows later snapshots, columns earlier, with interval and excluded counts), the directional statistic against its permutation null (histogram of the 720 permuted values with the observed one marked), the control strip (pass/fail per control, with the weakened-copy and starved-arena cells shown), and the naive metric next to the sealed one for every fixture, so a visitor sees where the naive reading is fooled. Link from `/opponents/` back and forth. Page reads `evidence.json` only and imports nothing from sim-ref. Private: covered by the first rung's Caddy rule `^/opponents(?:/|$)` and the `Dockerfile.site` `dist/opponents` removal; the new Vite chunk is named `opponents-archive` so the existing `assets/opponents-*` removal patterns catch it.
   887	    25	
   888	    26	## 3. Design
   889	    27	
   890	    28	### 3.1 Schedule and seeds (frozen before any outcome)
   891	    29	- Evidence seeds 1001..1010 (disjoint from search seeds 1..300 recorded in the sources, screen 901..903, first rung 101..110 and calibration 1..3). Reserve block B, seeds 2001..2010, used only if an analysis-affecting amendment follows the freeze. Balanced arm only (rare starts are known biased against the rare side, even for identical genomes, so they are not run).
   892	    30	- Per source with K snapshots: `K(K-1)/2 = 10` ordered pairs x 10 seeds x 2 positions = 200 contests; solo vigour per snapshot x 2 masks x 10 seeds = 100 runs; controls: twin 20, weakened copy 20, starved 20. About 360 runs per source; three sources plus planted-chain (6 pairs, 120 contests) and strength-not-time (reuses the first rung's panel genomes, 10 pairs, 200 contests) is about 1500 runs. The first rung cost about 1.8 CPU-seconds per run (724 runs, 1290 CPU-s); expect about 2700 CPU-s, 6 to 12 minutes at 8 workers on a quiet machine. The 90-minute wall limit does not bound CPU (8 workers allows about 12 CPU-hours), so the budget is cumulative CPU: viability screen (about 450 s), write (about 2500 s), replay (about 2500 s), mini and regeneration tests, EVIDENCE bookkeeping; the CLI counts CPU-seconds per command and aborts past 3 CPU-hours for any one command and the slice records the total in `EVIDENCE.md` (target under 2 CPU-hours total). `uptime` is checked first and `--jobs` reduced when load > 40; replay-check may replay a frozen deterministic 25% sample of jobs plus full hash verification if the full replay would exceed the budget (recorded). Cut order if over budget: third source first, in-world strength-not-time rerun, then residual statistic.
   893	    31	
   894	    32	### 3.2 Statistics
   895	    33	- Per ordered pair: block payoff, interval, sign-flip p, status `informative`/`uninformative`, exactly as the first rung (`summarizePair`, complete blocks only). Everything below is DESCRIPTIVE/exploratory. The only confirmatory gates are the instrument controls (twin, calibrated weakened copy, starved arena, planted chain, strength-not-time and cycle fixtures); there is no per-source significance test and no multiplicity claim, so the earlier Bonferroni problem (a 4-snapshot source cannot reach p below .05/3 since its minimum is 1/24) does not arise.
   896	    34	- Directional statistic `D` = mean over (later i, earlier j) pairs of `(payoff_ij - 0.5)`, informative pairs only, count reported. Descriptive rank `r_dir = #{D_perm >= D_obs} / K!` over every permutation of the K time labels over the snapshots (K! = 120 for K = 5, 24 for a 4-snapshot source; the identity counts itself, so the minimum is 1/K!). The permutation moves each snapshot together with its whole eligibility mask and pair outcomes, so informative-pair exclusion does not change the support. It is a rank against relabelled times, not a p-value: the selected, genealogically related archive is not exchangeable, so it is reported as `r_dir` and never called significant. The reverse tail `r_rev` is reported the same way. Sub-labels use thresholds frozen in config: `r_dir <= 0.05` is `directional-rank`.
   897	    35	- Edges for cycles use only `PairSummary.edge` confirmed edges (the first rung's full rule: every retained block on one side, at least nine retained blocks, corrected significance, effect threshold) and a general directed-cycle detector (any length), reported as a flag independent of the temporal label.
   898	    36	- Vigour residual (descriptive): solo-growth-predicted share vs observed, as in the first rung, so a "win" that is just general vigour is flagged; the directional statistic is also reported on the residual.
   899	    37	
   900	    38	### 3.3 Verdict table (frozen, precedence in order; two separate outputs per source)
   901	    39	Temporal label (one per source, first match wins):
   902	    40	1. Any instrument control invalid (label invariance not exact, a run invalid, starved arena not `both-floor`) -> `control-invalid`.
   903	    41	2. Twin position effect fails or the calibrated weakened-copy control is not detected (all retained blocks below 0.5, mean <= 0.40, as in the first rung's thresholds, read from its `config.json`/evidence rather than restated) -> `insensitive-or-biased`.
   904	    42	3. Planted-chain not `directional-rank`, or `strength-not-time` scrambled not `ordered-not-temporal`, or `vigour-only` not `directional-vigour-only` (global, run once) -> `assay-failed`, no source label.
   905	    43	4. Any scheduled pair `uninformative` (denominator is all scheduled pairs, as the first rung's gate rejects any uninformative pair) -> `partial-tournament`, with the informative count; labels 5-7 are then computed on the informative pairs and flagged partial.
   906	    44	5. `r_dir <= 0.05`: if the vigour-residual `r_dir` is also <= 0.05 -> `directional-rank`; else `directional-vigour-only`.
   907	    45	6. A strength ordering exists (>= 3 `PairSummary.edge` edges forming a transitive order) but `r_dir > 0.05` -> `ordered-not-temporal`.
   908	    46	7. Otherwise -> `nothing-beyond-null`, stated as "none detected at this resolution", never "none exists".
   909	    47	Graph flag (independent): `cycle` when the confirmed-edge digraph has a directed cycle of any length, else `acyclic`; stated alongside the temporal label, never instead of it (a source can be `directional-rank` and `cycle`; the page says so).
   910	    48	Across sources: counts per label, no pooled rank. Related histories do not replicate one another; two sources agreeing is described as "similar in related searches", nothing stronger.
   911	    49	
   912	    50	### 3.4 Freeze-before-run (D3 and the stage contract)
   913	    51	Files committed and pushed to the branch before any scoring, nothing from `evidence.json`/`manifest.json` in that commit: this PLAN, `archive-config.json` (sources, bin rule, K, seeds, floor, thresholds, verdict table version), `archive.json` snapshot fixture (selected hexes and hashes), the library and CLI code that defines the schedule and rules, and their tests on the fixtures. The viability screen uses screen seeds 901..903 only (not a scoring, calibration or search seed) and its outputs are inside the frozen `archive.json`. The freeze commit holds the PLAN, config, archive fixture, library, CLI and tests; the manifest records the freeze commit SHA and a frozen inventory (path -> SHA-256) of every scientific file (library, CLI, config, archive.json, and the first-rung files it depends on). `--freeze-check` verifies the commit is an ancestor of HEAD, that every inventoried file is byte-identical to that commit, and that no outcome files were in it; a test mutates one library line after a temp-repo freeze and the check fails. Any analysis-affecting amendment after outcomes exist requires fresh scoring seeds (block B) and marks the old result exploratory-superseded. Own Codex review (Sol 6.1, xhigh, read-only) of the freeze files happens before the freeze commit. Anything changed after the freeze is a dated amendment in `EVIDENCE.md`.

   985	   123	
   986	   124	## 8. Non-goals and limits
   987	   125	
   988	   126	- No evolving against an archive, no recent-only/fixed-panel/mixture comparison, no withheld-opponent generality, no whole-patch or ecological-context transfer, no predation or reproduction claim, no open-endedness claim.
   989	   127	- Archive time is search evaluation index; archives are surviving elites, not a full history; sources are three search runs, so intervals are wide and "nothing" means none detected at this resolution.
   990	   128	- Biomass share at step 300 in a small CPU arena with a single chemistry; balanced arm only; no GPU or physical-hardware evidence.
   991	   129	- No physics, schema, preset, `RULE_VERSION` or `defaultConfig()` change; no new `WorldConfig` keys; no paid calls, AWS, deploy; the plan stage commits nothing.
   992	   130	- If scope must shrink, cut in this order: third source, `strength-not-time` in-world version (keep the pure matrix), the Playwright spec (keep render tests), residual-based statistic.
   993	   131	
   994	   132	## 9. Order of work
   995	   133	
   996	   134	1. Library with pure functions and tests (selection, permutation null, verdict, fixtures). 2. Snapshot selection and viability screen from `RUNS` into `archive.json` and `archive-config.json`. 3. CLI (`--freeze-check`, mini config). 4. Codex review of freeze files, fix, commit only freeze files, `git push -u origin claude/i03-archive-tournament`. 5. `--write` the evidence, `--replay-check`. 6. Page, packaging edits, render and e2e tests, screenshots. 7. `EVIDENCE.md` (result, deviations, cost), full gates, Codex source review.

   998	   136	## v3 pre-freeze amendment (2026-11-02, IMPLEMENT stage, before any scoring seed was run)
   999	   137	
  1000	   138	Made after chain calibration on screen seeds 901..903 only (`chain-calibration.json`, 7 trials) and before the freeze commit. No scoring seed (1001..1010) had been run.
  1001	   139	
  1002	   140	1. **Planted-chain gate (section 2.2 and verdict row 3).** v2 required the planted chain to be `directional-rank`. Six of seven calibration trials reached `r_dir` at the K=4 floor (1/24; the seventh, `[70,45,20,0]`, was 2/24 and `nothing-beyond-null`) and none had an aligned vigour residual (residual ranks 0.29 to 0.67), because the planted manipulation (a weakened photosynthesis weight) is a vigour manipulation by construction. The gate is now: planted chain is `directional-rank` or `directional-vigour-only` (it must be directional; the vigour split is gated by the `vigour-only` fixture). Selected chain: weakening percents `[80,60,40,0]`, time in ascending strength.
  1003	   141	2. **Stated consequence.** No fixture or control reaches `directional-rank` (strength-aligned on the first rung's panel is also `directional-vigour-only`, residual rank 0.092). The top label has no positive control; a real `directional-rank` verdict would be unvalidated and is recorded as a limit in the evidence.
  1004	   142	3. **strength-not-time in-world rerun is cut** (allowed by section 8's cut order); the pure-matrix fixture on the first rung's committed matrix is the gate. Scrambled order frozen in `archive-config.json`: `F3,F2,G,F1,W` (rank 0.25, naive later-wins fraction 0.7, ordered-not-temporal).
  1005	   143	4. **Vigour residual directional statistic** uses centre 0 (later minus earlier residual), not 0.5; implemented in `permutationRank(m, centre)`.
  1006	   144	5. **Selection outcome** (screen seeds 901..903, 13 genomes screened, all passed): all three sources kept with five viable bins each (`bootstrap-m3` horizon 80, `bootstrap-200` horizon 298, `bootstrap-div` horizon 80). The first-ranked elite of bin 2 of `bootstrap-m3` is byte-identical to bin 0 of `bootstrap-200` and bin 2 of `bootstrap-div` (flagged `shared-genome`; none of the three sources is wholly shared, so none is dropped). Elite `quality` is saturated near 1.0 for most elites, so ties (broken by cell then born) decide many bins.
  1007	   145	6. **Ordering of "ordered" strength**: `ordered` means no directed cycle among confirmed edges and at least 3 confirmed edges (a topological strength order exists).
  1008	   146	7. **Review-driven changes before freeze (Sol xhigh freeze review, 8 findings)**: the freeze inventory now includes `stats.ts`, all schema and sim-ref sources, PLAN.md and chain-calibration.json; `--check` verifies freeze provenance (ancestor, inventory, manifest commit); `--replay-check` runs the full saved-evidence and freeze verification before any simulation and refuses without evidence; the planted-chain gate requires every scheduled chain pair informative; viability flags in `archive.json` are recomputed from the recorded counts; test-only seeds 7001, 7002 replace the reserved scoring seeds in pre-freeze tests.

  1050	# CLAUDE.md
  1051	
  1052	This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.
  1053	
  1054	## What this is
  1055	
  1056	An open-ended artificial-life ecology on WebGPU: closed in matter, open in energy, exact integer physics. Individuals, reproduction and heredity are *inferred* from local rules, never declared (no `Organism` class, no `reproduce()`). Research goal and milestones (M3 bootstrap → M4/M5 ensembles): `docs/plan.md`. Rules in prose plus the arithmetic-bounds argument: `docs/rules.md`.
  1057	
  1058	The repo is a pnpm workspace (TypeScript packages + Vite lab), a Deno toolchain for headless GPU runs, and a Phoenix app (`apps/coordinator`). It is jj-colocated with git (`.jj/`).
  1059	
  1060	## Commands
  1061	
  1062	```bash
  1063	pnpm install
  1064	pnpm dev                 # lab at http://localhost:5173 (/selftest.html, /island.html)
  1065	bin/dev                  # lab + coordinator together (PORT, LAB_PORT override 4000/5173)
  1066	pnpm typecheck           # tsc over packages/*/src, apps/lab/src, tests (excludes tests/deno)
  1067	pnpm test                # vitest: apps/lab/test, packages/*/test, experiments/test, tools/test
  1068	pnpm vitest run packages/sim-ref/test/ref.test.ts -t "<name>"   # single test
  1069	deno run -A tests/deno/gpu_golden.ts    # GPU vs CPU reference, bit for bit (native WebGPU)
  1070	npx playwright test      # browser golden (tests/e2e), Chrome with WebGPU flags; BL_ALL_ENGINES=1 adds firefox/webkit
  1071	BL_E2E_PORT=5241 npx playwright test   # a second worktree on its own port (default 5174; strictPort, never reuses a server)
  1072	cd apps/coordinator && mix test         # or `mix test test/coordinator/queue_test.exs`
  1073	cd apps/coordinator && mix precommit    # warnings-as-errors, format, test
  1074	pnpm gen:prereg          # regenerate experiments/preregistration.md generated sections
  1075	pnpm gen:cards           # redraw the link-preview cards (site + one per world) with Chrome/WebGPU; then look at them
  1076	```
  1077	
  1078	Run `pnpm install` before any `deno run` in a fresh checkout (deno 2.9 otherwise writes `workspaces` into package.json). Tests that start deno pass `--no-lock` (`tools/test/deno-no-lock.test.ts` checks; a launcher or coordinator a test starts that runs deno itself gets the PATH shim in `tests/deno/lib/no_lock_deno.ts`), but a top-level `deno run`, or a tool that starts deno, still writes a stale deno.lock: when package.json deps change, update it deliberately (`deno cache --frozen tools/stitch.ts` fails while it is stale).
  1079	
  1080	`tests/deno/*.ts` are standalone scripts (not `Deno.test`); run each with `deno run -A tests/deno/<name>.ts`. Several need a real GPU; `coordinator_integration.ts` spawns `mix phx.server` against a scratch data dir.
  1081	
  1082	Experiment CLIs (`tools/`, all Deno, native WebGPU): `run.ts` (write run bundles to `runs/<experiment>/<preset>/<condition>/seed-<n>/`), `analyze.ts` (ensemble analysis), `bootstrap.ts` (M3 MAP-Elites search), `retest.ts`, `calibrate.ts`, `island.ts` (headless island), `stitch.ts` (coordinator segments → bundles). Usage is in each file's header comment and in README.md. `runs/` is gitignored and can hold very large outputs.
  1083	
  1084	## Architecture
  1085	
  1086	**Package imports.** `@bl/*` resolve to `packages/*/src/index.ts` via four separate maps that must stay in sync: `tsconfig.json` paths, `vitest.config.ts` aliases, `deno.json` imports, and `apps/lab/vite.config.ts` aliases. There is no build step for packages.
  1087	
  1088	**Two implementations of one rule set.**
  1089	- `packages/sim-ref/src/step.ts` is the executable specification (CPU).
  1090	- `packages/sim-gpu/src/shaders.ts` (WGSL) must match it bit for bit. `packages/sim-gpu/src/golden.ts` defines golden cases and runs on any WebGPU host (Deno, browser `/selftest.html`, Playwright).
  1091	- `packages/sim-ref/test/golden-hashes.test.ts` pins state hashes per `RULE_VERSION` (in `packages/schema/src/config.ts`). Any change to dynamics changes the hashes: an intended rule change bumps `RULE_VERSION` and re-pins deliberately. A change that isn't meant to alter dynamics must leave every pin untouched — new optional `WorldConfig` keys stay absent from `defaultConfig()` so existing configs hash identically.
  1092	- All arithmetic is integer (u32 matter, fixed-point controllers, counter-based PRNG keyed on seed/step/cell, stochastic rounding with remainders to the source). Conservation residual is exactly zero. Keep new arithmetic inside the bounds listed in `docs/rules.md` (`MATTER_MAX`, `POOL_MAX`, `mulShr` limits); `validateConfig`/`validateState` enforce them.
  1093	
  1094	**Packages.** `schema` (config, presets, layout, genome, worlds, checkpoint encoding, migration/exchange, M3 founder set) → `sim-ref`, `sim-gpu` → `metrics` (census, tracker, collectives, activity, complexity, stats; runs on readbacks) → `runner` (conditions, `runExperiment`, observers, stitching, island client) and `search` (MAP-Elites, gates). `lineage` (schema, sim-ref) is the lineage inspector's genotype core: keys, ancestry, mutation replay, the controller probe, and the mutation-edge store the lab keeps. `runner` code runs unchanged in browsers and Deno.
  1095	
  1096	**Checkpoints and replay.** A checkpoint artifact carries physics state and observer state together, under one digest. Segmented runs must equal a continuous run byte for byte (`tests/deno/segments.ts`, `tests/deno/stitch.ts`); this determinism is what makes distributed verification possible.
  1097	
  1098	**Archipelago.** `apps/coordinator` (Phoenix, JSON API under `/api`, static status page `priv/static/index.html`) hands islands segment tasks. `Coordinator.Queue` does scheduling, `Coordinator.Segment` owns segment state transitions, and `Coordinator.Store` is content-addressed storage for checkpoints (`.blck`) and bundle files. A fraction of segments is re-run by a second island and compared by state hash. Islands are `apps/lab/island.html` (browser) or `tools/island.ts` (Deno), both via `packages/runner/src/island.ts`. `apps/coordinator/AGENTS.md` has Phoenix/Elixir/OTP conventions to follow there; everything between its `usage-rules` markers is generated by `mix usage_rules.sync` (config in `mix.exs`), so rerun that after dependency changes rather than editing the section. `mix usage_rules.search_docs "<term>" -p <pkg>` searches hexdocs for the coordinator's deps.
  1099	
  1100	**Lab.** `apps/lab/src/sim.worker.ts` owns the WebGPU device, frame loop, rendering and OPFS storage. `apps/lab/src/execution.ts` owns census cadence, observer integrity, checkpoint settlement and replay traversal. State-changing messages run through `exclusive()`, which waits for in-flight frame execution; generation identity invalidates stale readbacks.
  1101	
  1102	**Pre-registration.** `experiments/endpoints.ts` is the source of truth for primary endpoints, held-out observables and activity thresholds, and `tools/analyze.ts` executes it. The generated sections of `experiments/preregistration.md` (between `GENERATED` markers) come from `pnpm gen:prereg`, and `experiments/test/prereg-sync.test.ts` fails when they are stale. Edit `endpoints.ts` and regenerate; never hand-edit inside the markers. The doc is frozen (2026-09-27): its SHA-256 is recorded in `experiments/FROZEN`, `pnpm gen:prereg` now refuses to rewrite the generated sections, and every change goes in a dated amendment section.
  1103	
  1104	**Analysis scale.** Run bundles can be hundreds of MB per run (for example `lineages.tsv`). Analysis tools stream per census rather than loading whole tables; keep new analysis code streaming.
  1105	
  1106	## Codex reviews
  1107	
  1108	Reviews by Codex use **Sol 6.1 at High effort**, not Astra (standing instruction from 2026-10-03, replaces the earlier `gpt-6-astra` policy). Run them read-only at phase boundaries and always before committing:
  1109	
  1110	```bash
  1111	codex exec -m gpt-6.1-sol -c model_reasoning_effort=high -s read-only -C <checkout> - < prompt.md > review.txt
  1112	```
  1113	
  1114	Use `< /dev/null` when the prompt is an argument (otherwise codex waits on stdin) and `-o <file>` for the final message. Historical "Astra review" comments in the code are provenance for past reviews; leave them. Delegated agents and workspaces follow the same rule.

  1256	     6	
  1257	     7	- Starved arena margin: on test seeds 7001 and 7002 the 300-step starved probe of `m3:0` vs `m3:1` ended with label ratios 0.045 to 0.053 against the floor 0.0625 (1/16), a thin margin. If any of the 20 scoring runs stays above the floor, `starvedOk` is false, every source is recorded `control-invalid`, and a different depletion pair or override is a next-rung item, not a block-B amendment in this slice.
  1258	     8	- If a library bug surfaces during or after `--write`, it is recorded here and not fixed in the frozen files; the outcome stands as written, or the slice goes to block B (seeds 2001..2010) with the old result marked exploratory-superseded.

Disclosure from REVIEW.md: an early mini and arena test ran 60-step worlds with scoring seeds 1001 and 1002 before the test-only seeds were introduced; outputs were not read as outcomes.

  1266	    16	
  1267	    17	Global controls: runs valid; identical-genome swap exact in all three sources; calibrated weakened copy G vs W detected (mean W share 0.325, 10 of 10 blocks below 0.5, alive alone); starved arena `m3:0` vs `m3:1` both labels below the floor in all 20 runs (max ratio 0.054 against 0.0625, the pre-registered thin margin held), status uninformative; all five pure fixtures and the planted chain (six in all) passed (`assayOk` true).
  1268	    18	
  1269	    19	How to read it:
  1270	    20	- `m3` and `b200` are masked by the twin position control only: mean place effect 0.092 and 0.093 against the first rung's target 0.075 (a magnitude failure), sign-flip p 0.63 and 0.35 (no systematic direction); `div` passed it (0.057, p 0.082). The target was calibrated on the generalist; the latest archive genomes show a larger place effect in this arena. A genome-specific target or position-adjusted payoff is a next-rung item, not an amendment. No `positionAdvantage` entry is null.
  1271	    21	- `b200` numbers are masked, not a label, and descriptive only: D = 0.196, r_dir 0.033 (floor 0.008), residual rank 0.15, naive later-wins 90%, 8 confirmed edges, acyclic. Not "improved": the frozen table fires row 2 and computes no body label. This is the case where the naive metric reads 90% and the control strip stops the claim. The largest pair offset (`b200:1` vs `b200:4`, mean 0.085, offset 0.415) exceed the measured place effect (0.09) but the rule is a gate, not an adjustment.
  1272	    22	- `m3`: D -0.026, r_dir 0.69, naive 30%, 3 edges, acyclic (masked too). `div`: D -0.004, r_dir 0.55, residual rank 0.30, 4 confirmed edges, acyclic: a strength order exists and does not follow search time.
  1273	    23	- No cycle in any source: none detected at this resolution.
  1274	    24	- Weakened copies of each source's latest snapshot did not lose (mean share 0.50, 0.52, 0.48): the plan review's finding 9 realised, the photosynthesis-weight edit is generalist-specific. Descriptive only; the calibrated G/W pair carried the gate.
  1275	    25	- No fixture or control reaches `directional-rank` (strength-aligned and planted chain are `directional-vigour-only`); the top label is unvalidated.
  1276	    26	- Answer to the question: nothing shows directional improvement against the past under the frozen rules; the only strong later-beats-earlier pattern (b200) is blocked by a failed position control, one source shows a strength order unrelated to time, and no cycles were found.

  1579	    41	    ['Planted chain', 'Graded weakening of one organism, in order, must be called directional (rank or vigour-only).', `${S.chain.fixture.label}; rank ${rank(S.chain.fixture.rDir)}`, S.chain.fixture.pass],
  1580	    42	  ];
  1581	    43	  el('controls').querySelector('tbody')!.innerHTML = rows.map(([a, b, c, ok]) => `<tr><th scope="row">${esc(a)}</th><td>${esc(b)}</td><td>${esc(c)}</td><td>${ok === null ? 'n/a (descriptive)' : ok ? 'yes' : '<strong>no</strong>'}</td></tr>`).join('');
  1582	    44	  const nrows = [

  1589	    51	function render() {
  1590	    52	  const s = S.sources.find((x) => x.short === source.value) as Src;
  1591	    53	  const t = s.tournament, a = t.analysis;
  1592	    54	  const masked = s.label === 'control-invalid' || s.label === 'insensitive-or-biased';
  1593	    55	  const failed = [!s.twin.labelInvariance ? 'identical-organism swap not exact' : '', !s.twin.positionOk ? `identical-organism place effect too large (mean size ${s.twin.meanAbs === null ? '–' : f2(s.twin.meanAbs)} against a target of ${s.twin.effectTarget.toFixed(3)}, sign-flip p ${s.twin.signFlipP === null ? '–' : s.twin.signFlipP.toFixed(2)})` : '', !S.gw.detected ? 'weakened copy not caught' : '', !S.global.starvedOk ? 'starved arena not unreadable' : ''].filter(Boolean);
  1594	    56	  el('srchelp').textContent = `Label for this search: ${s.label}${s.underlying ? ` (underlying: ${s.underlying})` : ''}: ${LABEL_TEXT[s.label] ?? Informative pairs: ${a.informative} of ${a.scheduled}.`;
  1595	    57	  const cells = laterVsEarlier(t.ids, t.pairs);
  1596	    58	  el('mcap').textContent = `Share of biomass the row (later) snapshot holds against the column (earlier) snapshot at step 300, with a 95% interval across seed blocks and the blocks kept. 0.50 is an even split. Uninformative pairs are shown as such and never read as a tie. Time runs down the rows and across the columns.`;
  1597	    59	  el('matrix').querySelector('thead')!.innerHTML = `<tr><th scope="col">Later against earlier</th>${t.ids.map((id) => `<th scope="col">${esc(id)}</th>`).join('')}</tr>`;
  1598	    60	  el('matrix').querySelector('tbody')!.innerHTML = cells.map((row, i) => `<tr><th scope="row">${esc(t.ids[i])}</th>${row.map((c) => `<td class="cell ${c.state}" style="background:${shade(c.value)}">${c.state === 'uninformative' ? '<strong>uninformative</strong><br>' : ''}${esc(c.text)}</td>`).join('')}</tr>`).join('');
  1599	    61	  const obs = a.rank.observed;
  1600	    62	  el('nulltext').textContent = `Directional statistic D = the mean, over informative pairs, of the later snapshot's share minus 0.5. Observed D = ${obs === null ? 'n/a' : obs.toFixed(3)}. Against all ${a.rank.perms} relabelled timelines the observed one ranks ${rank(a.rank.rDir)} (smallest possible ${rank(a.rank.minRank)}); the reverse tail is ${rank(a.rank.rRev)}. This is a rank, not a p-value. With the growing-alone residual the rank is ${rank(a.residRank?.rDir ?? null)}.`;
  1601	    63	  const h = histogram(t.permuted, obs);
  1602	    64	  const max = Math.max(...h.counts, 1), bw = 440 / h.counts.length;
  1603	    65	  el('hist').innerHTML = `<rect x="0" y="0" width="480" height="160" fill="none"/>` + h.counts.map((c, i) => `<rect class="${i === h.observedBin ? 'obs' : 'bar'}" x="${20 + i * bw}" y="${130 - (110 * c) / max}" width="${bw - 1}" height="${(110 * c) / max}"><title>${c} timelines${i === h.observedBin ?   1604	    66	    `<text x="20" y="148">${h.lo.toFixed(3)}</text><text x="460" y="148" text-anchor="end">${h.hi.toFixed(3)}</text><text x="240" y="12" text-anchor="middle">gold bar: observed D = ${obs === null ? 'n/a' : obs.toFixed(3)}</text>`;
  1605	    67	  el('histcap').textContent = `Histogram of D over all ${a.rank.perms} relabelled timelines (${t.permuted.length} with an informative statistic). The gold bar contains the observed D.`;
  1606	    68	}

  1612	     1	// Pure view helpers for the archive-tournament page. Reads numbers from evidence.json only; imports nothing from sim-ref.
  1613	     2	export const esc = (s: string): string => s.replace(/[&<>"']/g, (c) => ({ '&': '&amp;', '<': '&lt;', '>': '&gt;', '"': '&quot;', "'": '&#39;' })[c] as string);
  1614	     3	export const f2 = (x: number): string => x.toFixed(2);
  1615	     4	
  1616	     5	export interface PairView {
  1617	     6	  a: string; b: string;
  1618	     7	  summary: { complete: number; excluded: number; blocks: number; mean: number | null; interval: { lo: number; hi: number } | null; status: 'informative' | 'uninformative'; edge: string | null };
  1619	     8	}
  1620	     9	export interface Cell { state: 'self' | 'empty' | 'informative' | 'uninformative'; text: string; value: number | null }
  1621	    10	
  1622	    11	/** Matrix with rows later snapshots and columns earlier ones: the cell is the row snapshot's share against the column snapshot. */
  1623	    12	export function laterVsEarlier(ids: string[], pairs: PairView[]): Cell[][] {
  1624	    13	  const by = new Map(pairs.map((p) => [`${p.a}|${p.b}`, p]));
  1625	    14	  return ids.map((row, i) => ids.map((col, j) => {
  1626	    15	    if (i === j) return { state: 'self', text: '', value: null } as Cell;
  1627	    16	    if (j > i) return { state: 'empty', text: '', value: null } as Cell; // the column is later than the row: shown in the other triangle
  1628	    17	    const p = by.get(`${col}|${row}`) as PairView; // stored as earlier|later, payoff is the earlier one's
  1629	    18	    const s = p.summary;
  1630	    19	    const ex = s.excluded ? `, ${s.excluded} of ${s.blocks} blocks excluded` : '';
  1631	    20	    if (s.status === 'uninformative' || s.mean === null) return { state: 'uninformative', text: `uninformative (${s.complete} of ${s.blocks} blocks kept${ex ? '' : ''})`, value: null } as Cell;
  1632	    21	    const m = 1 - s.mean;
  1633	    22	    const iv = s.interval ? ` [${f2(1 - s.interval.hi)}, ${f2(1 - s.interval.lo)}]` : '';
  1634	    23	    return { state: 'informative', text: `${f2(m)}${iv}, n=${s.complete}${ex}${s.edge ? ', confirmed edge' : ''}`, value: m } as Cell;
  1635	    24	  }));
  1636	    25	}
  1637	    26	
  1638	    27	export interface Histogram { lo: number; hi: number; counts: number[]; observedBin: number }
  1639	    28	/** Histogram of the permuted statistic with the observed value's bin. */
  1640	    29	export function histogram(values: number[], observed: number | null, bins = 24): Histogram {
  1641	    30	  const lo = Math.min(...values, observed ?? Infinity), hi = Math.max(...values, observed ?? -Infinity);
  1642	    31	  const w = (hi - lo) / bins || 1;
  1643	    32	  const at = (v: number) => Math.min(bins - 1, Math.floor((v - lo) / w));
  1644	    33	  const counts = new Array(bins).fill(0);
  1645	    34	  for (const v of values) counts[at(v)]++;
  1646	    35	  return { lo, hi, counts, observedBin: observed === null ? -1 : at(observed) };
  1647	    36	}
  1648	    37	
  1649	    38	export function shade(v: number | null): string {
  1650	    39	  if (v === null) return 'transparent';
  1651	    40	  const t = Math.max(0, Math.min(1, Math.abs(v - 0.5) / 0.3));
  1652	    41	  return v >= 0.5 ? `rgba(94,160,120,${0.12 + 0.5 * t})` : `rgba(190,120,80,${0.12 + 0.5 * t})`;
  1653	    42	}
  1654	    43	export const pct = (x: number): string => `${Math.round(100 * x)}%`;
  1655	    44	export const rank = (x: number | null): string => (x === null ? 'n/a' : x.toFixed(3));

  1680	    17	      <p>Three searches each kept one best surviving organism per cell. We took five of them per search, spread over the search's own timeline, and played every later one against every earlier one in the first rung's small contest arena. We then ask whether the later ones win more than a shuffled timeline would say, whether the wins go in circles, or whether there is nothing beyond what shuffling gives.</p>
  1681	    18	      <p class="disclosure">CPU only, no GPU evidence, not the M3 environment. Local experiment, kept off the public site (noindex). A descriptive tournament of related survivors: the "time" is the search's batch number, the archive holds final survivors and not snapshots taken along the way, and the three searches are related, not independent. There is no significance claim and no pooled number.</p>

  1721	    58	      <ul>
  1722	    59	        <li>Archive time is the search's batch number, not ecological time and not an evaluation count. The archive is the final surviving set, so later survivors were bred from earlier ones and are not independent.</li>
  1723	    60	        <li>The rank against shuffled timelines is a rank, not a p-value: the survivors are related and not exchangeable. Nothing here is called significant, and the three searches are not replications of one another.</li>
  1724	    61	        <li>The searches rewarded survival, recovery and light dependence, not winning contests. A contest result is biomass share at step 300 on a small tile with a different light and chemistry than the searches used.</li>
  1725	    62	        <li>"Nothing beyond the null" means none detected at this resolution. Ten seed blocks give wide intervals.</li>
  1726	    63	        <li>No fixture reaches the top label: the first rung's aligned panel and the planted chain both missed the cutoff on the growing-alone residual rank (0.09 and 0.29, against 0.05), so they are labelled vigour-only and a real "directional-rank" would be unvalidated.</li>
  1727	    64	        <li>The cycle and vigour-only fixtures are synthetic. The weakened copy of an archive organism is descriptive only.</li>
  1728	    65	      </ul>

  1800	     5	const pair = (a: string, b: string, mean: number | null, over: Partial<PairView['summary']> = {}): PairView => ({
  1801	     6	  a, b, summary: { complete: 10, excluded: 0, blocks: 10, mean, interval: mean === null ? null : { lo: mean - 0.02, hi: mean + 0.02 }, status: mean === null ? 'uninformative' : 'informative', edge: null, ...over },
